Research Team

A solver, an inventor and a verifier work on each question. Methods are grouped by subject and topic so you can revise one topic at a time.

"Original" means original to this lab - no world-first claim. Every published method passed an independent solve. Time is how long a prepared student needs, not model latency.

⚡ Fastest routes

Before working the long way: check the options for range, units or a single plausible value, then use the route with the lowest seconds. These are the ten quickest in the bank.

  1. ~1s · Last Digit Check — Which of these is a perfect square?
  2. ~1s · Mental Multiplication — What is the value of x in the equation x/2 = 7?
  3. ~1s · Power Rule — The volume of a cube with side 5 cm is:
  4. ~1s · Direct Recall — The probability of getting a head when a fair coin is tossed is:
  5. ~1s · Mental Math — Two resistors 4 and 6 are connected in series. The total resistance is:
  6. ~1s · Pattern Recall — The value of x^2 dx is:
  7. ~1s · Ignore Redundant Info — If the probability of an event A is 0.4 and of B is 0.5, and A and Bare independent, then P(A n B) is:
  8. ~1s · Eliminate Negative Bounds — The range of the function f(x) = x² is:
  9. ~1s · Direct Recall — Which gas is known as "Laughing Gas"?
  10. ~1s · Concept Recall — Which process is used to separate crude oil into useful fractions?

🔢 Mathematics 221

Data Interpretation - Cost Analysis 1

INVENTEDData Interpretation - Cost Analysisverified2026-11-22
In a government procurement tender for 'Project Yogi', the contractor declares that the total estimated Cost Price (CP) for a batch of uniforms was calculated as 80% of the total Selling Price (SP). If the final SP for the batch was Rs. 240,000, and the actual expenses incurred were: Materials (40% of CP), Labor (35% of CP), and Overheads (25% of CP), what is the amount spent on Overheads? **Note:** The 'Materials' expense was found to be under-estimated by Rs. 5,000 in the initial draft, but the question asks for the **calculated** Overhead based on the declared percentages and the given SP.
Rs. 48,000Rs. 53,000Rs. 52,000Rs. 60,000
Answer: Rs. 48,000
Direct SP Ratio Method~10salternative
  1. Combine percentages: Overheads = 25% of CP = 25% of (80% of SP) = 20% of SP.
  2. Calculate 20% of Rs. 240,000 = Rs. 48,000.
  3. Match with option 1: Rs. 48,000.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab

Elementary Mathematics 5

SSC-GDElementary Mathematicsverified2026-11-15
The difference between Compound Interest (compounded annually) and Simple Interest on a certain sum for 2 years at 10% per annum is Rs. 120. Find the principal sum.
A. Rs. 12,000B. Rs. 10,000C. Rs. 15,000D. Rs. 8,000
Answer: A
Difference Formula~5sshortcut

CI-SI for 2 years equals P*(r/100)^2

  1. Difference = P*(10/100)^2 = P/100
  2. Set P/100 = 120 => P = 12000
  3. Select option A
More methods (2)
Interest on Interest~5smnemonic

Difference is interest earned on first year's interest

  1. First year interest = 10% of P = 0.1P
  2. Interest on that = 10% of 0.1P = 0.01P = 120
  3. P = 120/0.01 = 12000
  4. Option A
Direct SI & CI~6sinvariant

Compute both interests and subtract

  1. SI = P*10*2/100 = 0.2P
  2. CI = P(1.1)^2 - P = 0.21P
  3. Difference = 0.01P = 120 => P=12000
  4. Option A
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
SSC-GDElementary Mathematicsverified2026-11-18
If the numerator of a fraction is increased by 20% and the denominator is decreased by 10%, the fraction becomes 4/5. What was the original fraction?
A. 2/3B. 3/5C. 4/7D. 5/8
Answer: B
Ratio Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use combined change factor to directly compute original fraction

  1. Let original fraction be N/D.
  2. New fraction = (1.2N)/(0.9D) = (4/3)*(N/D).
  3. Set (4/3)*(N/D) = 4/5.
  4. Solve N/D = (4/5)*(3/4) = 3/5.
  5. Match option B.
More methods (2)
Scale Invariant~5sinvariant

Recognize the net multiplier on the fraction is constant

  1. Increasing numerator by 20% multiplies fraction by 1.2.
  2. Decreasing denominator by 10% multiplies fraction by 1/0.9 = 10/9.
  3. Combined factor = 1.2 * (10/9) = 4/3.
  4. Original fraction = (4/5) ÷ (4/3) = 3/5.
  5. Answer is option B.
Test Options~10sshortcut

Plug each choice into the percentage changes to see which yields 4/5

  1. For each option, multiply numerator by 1.2.
  2. Multiply denominator by 0.9.
  3. Simplify the resulting fraction.
  4. Check which equals 4/5.
  5. Option B (3/5) gives 3.6/4.5 = 4/5.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
SSC-GDElementary Mathematicsverified2026-11-18
The perimeter of a rectangle is 60 cm and its length is twice its breadth. What is the area of the rectangle?
A. 150 cm²B. 180 cm²C. 200 cm²D. 220 cm²
Answer: C
Perimeter Parts~3sshortcut

Split perimeter into 6 equal parts (2L+2B = 6B)

  1. Perimeter 60 ÷ 6 = 10 = breadth
  2. Length = 2 × 10 = 20
  3. Area = 20 × 10 = 200
More methods (2)
Half-Perimeter~4sshortcut

Half-perimeter = L+B = 3B, then multiply

  1. Half-perimeter = 60 ÷ 2 = 30
  2. 30 ÷ 3 = 10 = breadth
  3. Area = 20 × 10 = 200
Area Ratio~5sinvariant

Area = (P/6) × (P/3) = P²/18

  1. P² = 60² = 3600
  2. 3600 ÷ 18 = 200
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
SSC-GDElementary Mathematicsverified2026-11-22
Three partners A, B and C invest in a business. A invests ₹50,000 for 12 months, B invests ₹60,000 for 10 months and C invests ₹70,000 for 8 months. If the total profit at the end of the year is ₹44,000, what is B's share?
A. ₹12,000B. ₹15,000C. ₹14,000D. ₹13,000
Answer: B
Fraction of Total~8sshortcut

B's share = (B's investment-months ÷ total investment-months) × total profit.

  1. Total investment-months = 50,000×12 + 60,000×10 + 70,000×8 = 1,760,000
  2. B's investment-months = 600,000
  3. B's fraction = 600,000 ÷ 1,760,000 = 15/44
  4. Profit = 44,000 × 15/44 = 15,000
More methods (2)
Equal Product Shortcut~10sshortcut

A and B have equal investment-months, so they share equally; C's share follows ratio.

  1. Compute A: 50,000 × 12 = 600,000
  2. Compute B: 60,000 × 10 = 600,000
  3. Compute C: 70,000 × 8 = 560,000
  4. Ratio = 600:600:560 = 15:15:14
  5. Total parts = 44, profit = 44,000 → 1 part = 1,000
  6. B's share = 15 × 1,000 = 15,000
Ratio Simplification~10sshortcut

Divide by 1,000 then by 40 to get simplest integer ratio quickly.

  1. Divide investments by 1,000: 50, 60, 70
  2. Multiply by months: 50×12=600, 60×10=600, 70×8=560
  3. Divide all by 40: 15, 15, 14
  4. Sum = 44 parts
  5. B = (15/44) × 44,000 = 15,000
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
SSC-GDElementary Mathematicsverified2026-11-23
The difference between compound interest and simple interest on a certain sum for 2 years at 10% per annum is ₹ 125. Find the principal amount.
A. ₹ 10,000B. ₹ 12,500C. ₹ 15,000D. ₹ 20,000
Answer: B
Direct Formula Memory~4smnemonic

Memorize difference = P * R^2 / 10000 for 2 years

  1. 125 = P * 10^2 / 10000 = P * 100/10000 = P/100
  2. P = 125 * 100 = 12500
More methods (2)
Square Rate Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use the 2-year CI-SI difference formula P*(r/100)^2

  1. Difference = P * (10/100)^2 = P * 0.01
  2. 125 = P * 0.01 => P = 125 / 0.01 = 12500
Assume & Scale~8sestimate

Test a round principal, compute difference, then scale proportionally

  1. Assume P = 10000
  2. SI = 10000*10*2/100 = 2000
  3. CI = 10000*(1.1)^2 - 10000 = 2100
  4. Diff = 100
  5. Scale factor = 125/100 = 1.25 => P = 10000 * 1.25 = 12500
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper

Move constant, divide by coefficient

  3. Select option B which matches the result.

JEEMathematicsverified2026-11-08
The value of sin^230 degrees + cos^230 degrees is:
A. 0B. 1/2C. 1D. 3/4
Answer: C
Pythagorean Identity~2sinvariant

Recall fundamental trig rule

  1. Recognize 30° is same angle for both terms.
  2. Apply identity sin²θ + cos²θ = 1 directly.
  3. Conclude sum equals 1 regardless of θ value.
  4. Select option C
More methods (1)
Direct Calculation~4sshortcut

Evaluate individual values first

  1. Calculate sin(30°) = 0.5 and square it to get 0.25.
  2. Calculate cos(30°) ≈ 0.866 and square it to get 0.75.
  3. Add the two squares: 0.25 + 0.75 equals 1.
  4. Select option C

JEEMathematicsverified2026-11-08
If A = {1, 2, 3} and B = {2, 3, 4}, find A B.
A. {1}B. {2, 3}C. {2, 3, 4}D. {1, 2, 3, 4}
Answer: B
Direct Intersection~2sshortcut

List shared elements immediately

  1. Identify elements in set A: 1, 2, 3.
  2. Identify elements in set B: 2, 3, 4.
  3. Select only numbers present in both lists (2 and 3).
More methods (2)
Filter-Keep Logic~2sshortcut

Scan list B and keep only those also in A

  1. Take every element from set B one by one.
  2. Check if that element exists inside set A.
  3. Construct the new set containing only the kept elements (2, 3).
Visual Overlap Check~3smnemonic

Mentally overlay the two sets to see common items

  1. Write A = {1, 2, 3} and B = {2, 3, 4} side by side.
  2. Focus on the middle section where numbers align in both rows.
  3. Circle the matching digits found at those positions (2 and 3).

JEEMathematicsverified2026-11-08
If the probability of an event A is 0.4 and of B is 0.5, and A and B are independent, then P(A n B) is:
A. 0.9B. 0.1C. 0.2D. 0.02
Answer: C
Decimal Multiply~2sshortcut

Quick mental arithmetic

  1. Ignore decimals first: multiply 4 times 5 to get 20.
  2. Count total decimal places in original numbers (one for 0.4, one for 0.5).
  3. Place decimal point two spots left of result: 0.20 becomes 0.2.
  4. Select option C which equals 0.2.
More methods (2)
Independence Rule~3sinvariant

Multiply probabilities directly

  1. Recall independence implies P(A n B) = P(A) * P(B).
  2. Substitute given values: 0.4 multiplied by 0.5.
  3. Calculate product: 0.20 matches option C.
  4. Final check confirms result is 0.2.
Exclusion Check~4sestimate

Eliminate impossible sums

  1. Sum P(A) and P(B) to get 0.9, ruling out option A (union not intersection).
  2. Recognize independent events require multiplication, not subtraction.
  3. Estimate result must be smaller than both individual probabilities.
  4. Only options B and D are too small; C fits the product range.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
JEEMathematicsverified2026-11-08
Which of these is a perfect square?
A. 20B. 24C. 36D. 48
Answer: C
Last Digit Check~1sshortcut

Use last digit properties to quickly eliminate options

  1. List possible last digits of squares: 0, 1, 4, 5, 6, 9.
  2. Check option A (20): ends in 0, but 20 is not a square (4*5).
  3. Check option B (24): ends in 4, but 24 is not a square (4*6).
  4. Check option D (48): ends in 8, impossible for a perfect square.
  5. Only option C (36) remains and fits the pattern.
  6. Final answer is C.
More methods (2)
Tens Rule Filter~2sshortcut

Eliminate non-squares by checking tens digit pattern

  1. Recall perfect squares 1-40: 1,4,9,16,25,36,49.
  2. Notice squares between 20 and 40 must end in 6 (only 36).
  3. Eliminate 20, 24, and 48 as they do not match the required ending or range.
  4. Select 36 as the only valid perfect square.
  5. Final answer is C.
Square Root Estimate~3sestimate

Roughly estimate root to find nearest integer square

  1. Estimate sqrt(20): between 4 and 5, not integer.
  2. Estimate sqrt(24): between 4 and 5, not integer.
  3. Estimate sqrt(36): exactly 6, a perfect square.
  4. Estimate sqrt(48): between 6 and 7, not integer.
  5. Confirm only 36 is a perfect square.
  6. Final answer is C.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper

A. 3B. 5C. 5D. 7

The lab answer differs from the answer key in the paper; kept for review.

Middle Element~2sinvariant

Pick center of sorted list

  1. Sort data: 1,3,5,7,9 (already done).
  2. Count items N=5.
  3. Find middle index (N+1)/2 = 3.
  4. Read value at position 3: 5.
More methods (1)
Skip-Count~3sshortcut

Jump over pairs to center

  1. Pair outer numbers: (1,9) and (3,7).
  2. Identify remaining unpaired number.
  3. That single number is the median.
  4. Result is 5.

If the function f(x) = x^2 - 4x + 3 is given, what is the value of x for which f(x) is maximum or minimum?
A. 0B. 1C. 2D. 3
Answer: C
Vertex Formula~3sshortcut

Use symmetry of parabola

  1. Identify quadratic form ax^2+bx+c with a=1, b=-4.
  2. Calculate vertex x-coordinate using formula x = -b/(2a).
More methods (2)
Derivative Zero~4sinvariant

Set slope to zero instantly

  1. Compute derivative f'(x) = 2x - 4.
  2. Solve equation 2x - 4 = 0 for x.
  3. Verify result matches options.
Axis of Symmetry~5sestimate

Find middle of roots quickly

  1. Find roots by setting f(x)=0: (x-1)(x-3)=0 giving x=1,3.
  2. Recognize vertex lies exactly halfway between roots.
  3. Calculate midpoint (1+3)/2 = 2.

Answer: A · paper key: B

The Scientist and Verifier disagreed; this method is kept for review, not published as verified.

Table Check~4sestimate

Test options by plugging in the known point

  1. Pick Option A: y=2x+1. Plug x=1 to get y=3.
  2. Match result (3) with given point (1,3).
  3. Select Option A as correct match.
  4. Ignore other options without full check
More methods (1)
Point-Slope Formula~5sshortcut

Direct substitution using given point and slope

  1. Start with formula y = mx + b where m is 2.
  2. Substitute x=1 and y=3 into the equation.
  3. Solve for b: 3 = 2(1) + b, so b = 1.
  4. Write final equation as y = 2x + 1

JEEMathematicsverified2026-11-08
Find the derivative of f(x) = sin(x^2).
A. cos(x^2)B. 2x cos(x)C. 2x cos(x^2)D. x^2 cos(x^2)
Answer: C
Pattern Recall~4smnemonic

Recognize composite trig function form

  1. Recall pattern d/dx[sin(g(x))] = g'(x)cos(g(x)).
  2. Identify g(x)=x^2 so g'(x)=2x.
  3. Combine terms to write result 2x cos(x^2).
More methods (2)
Chain Rule Direct~5sinvariant

Derivative of sin(u) is cos(u)*u'

  1. Set u = x^2 and compute u' = 2x.
  2. Apply derivative rule: d/dx[sin(u)] = cos(u) * u'.
  3. Substitute back: cos(x^2) * 2x to get 2x cos(x^2).
Outer-Inner Split~6sshortcut

Separate outer sine from inner square

  1. Identify outer function sin() and inner function x^2.
  2. Differentiate outer: derivative of sin is cos (keep argument x^2).
  3. Multiply by inner derivative 2x to get 2x cos(x^2).
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper

More methods (2)

Remove singletons first

  1. Ignore numbers appearing once (2, 6, 9).
  2. Compare remaining counts: 4 appears twice, 7 appears thrice.

Tally frequency quickly

CTETMathematicsverified2026-11-08
What is the sum of interior angles of a pentagon?
A. 360°B. 450°C. 540°D. 720°
Answer: C
Triangle Split~3sinvariant

Divide shape into triangles

  1. Draw diagonals from one vertex to non-adjacent vertices.
  2. Count the resulting triangles (n-2, where n=5).
  3. Multiply triangle count by 180° (sum of angles in a triangle).
More methods (2)
Formula Plug~4smnemonic

Apply standard polygon formula

  1. Recall formula: Sum = (n - 2) × 180°.
  2. Substitute n=5 for the pentagon sides.
  3. Calculate result and match to option C.
Vertex Pairing~6sshortcut

Sum angles via exterior turn logic

  1. Visualize walking around the pentagon perimeter.
  2. Note total exterior turns equal 360°.
  3. Subtract exterior sum from 720° (sum of all interior+exterior for hexagon? No, use 5*180=900 minus exterior 360).
  4. Simpler: Use known fact pentagon has 540° directly from standard knowledge base.

CTETMathematicsverified2026-11-08
A confectioner sells 'Assorted Box' X (cost ₹220/kg) and 'Premium Box' Y (cost ₹320/kg). To reduce inventory of Y, he mixes 2 kg of X with 1 kg of Y to create 'Box Z'. If Box Z is sold at a 10% profit on its cost price, what is the profit percentage earned on the original cost of the 3 kg ingredients?
A. A: 13.33%B. B: 10%C. C: 6.67%D. D: 5%
Answer: B
Profit Invariant~3sinvariant

Realize total profit % equals target profit %

  1. Note that 'Box Z' is sold at exactly 10% profit.
  2. Since Box Z consists entirely of the mixed ingredients, its overall profit rate on those ingredients must equal the selling rate.
  3. Therefore, the profit on the original 3 kg ingredients is exactly 10%.
  4. Select Option B without calculating intermediate totals.
More methods (2)
Weighted Average Check~4sestimate

Verify no hidden costs alter the percentage

  1. Identify that profit is calculated strictly on the cost of ingredients used.
  2. Confirm the mixture (2kg X + 1kg Y) has no additional processing cost mentioned.
  3. Conclude that selling the mix at 10% profit implies the base ingredients also yield 10%.
  4. Select Option B based on this direct inference.
Direct Cost Ratio~5sshortcut

Calculate total cost and selling price directly

  1. Sum ingredient costs: (2*220) + (1*320) = ₹760.
  2. Add 10% profit to total cost: 760 * 1.10 = ₹836.
  3. Calculate profit percentage on original cost: (836 - 760)/760 ≈ 10%.
  4. Select Option B as the result matches the given profit margin.

Link 'area' to multiplication instantly

Check units to rule out wrong ops quickly

  3. Recognize that adding or subtracting lengths does not fill space.
  4. Confirm Length x Width is the correct operation.

CTETMathematicsverified2026-11-08
A fixed deposit scheme offers Simple Interest (SI) calculated on a daily basis. If the interest for one full year is Rs. 4,800, what will be the interest earned if the money is withdrawn after exactly one-third of that year? Assume a non-leap year.
A. ₹1,600B. ₹2,400C. ₹3,200D. ₹4,800
Answer: A
Direct Proportion~5sinvariant

SI scales linearly with time

  1. Recognize Simple Interest is directly proportional to time for a fixed rate.
  2. Calculate one-third of the annual interest: 4800 / 3 = 1600.
More methods (2)
Fraction Logic~5sshortcut

Withdrawal time is exactly 1/3 of total period

  1. Identify that 'one-third of a year' means t = T/3.
  2. Apply formula SI = (P*R*T)/100. Since SI_total corresponds to T, the new SI is simply SI_total * (T/3 / T).
  3. Compute 4800 * (1/3) = 1600.
Elimination Check~5sestimate

Rule out non-linear options quickly

  1. Simple Interest never compounds; it grows linearly.
  2. Divide total interest (4800) by 3 because the duration is 1/3 of a year.
  3. Result is 1600, which matches Option A.

The Scientist and Verifier disagreed; this method is kept for review, not published as verified.

plug into single equation

  2. Substitute values directly: (18 * 20 + 120) / 20.
  3. Simplify numerator: 360 + 120 = 480.
  4. Divide 480 by 20 to get final time.
More methods (2)

use proportional time

  1. Find original length ratio: (360 + 120) / 360 = 4/3.
  2. Since speed is constant, time scales linearly with distance.
  3. Multiply original time (18 s) by the ratio 4/3.
  4. Result is 24 seconds.
Speed to m/s~5sshortcut

convert speed fast

  1. Convert 72 km/h to m/s: multiply by 5/18 to get 20 m/s.
  2. Calculate original length: 20 m/s * 18 s = 360 meters.
  3. Add 120 meters to length for new total distance (480 meters).
  4. Divide new distance by speed: 480 / 20 = 24 seconds.

Final Price Ratio~10sshortcut

Calculate final price directly then derive profit ratio

  4. Calculate profit as (Final SP - Cost) / Final SP: (1980 - 1760) / 1980.
  5. Simplify fraction 220/1980 to 11/99, which equals 1/9 or approximately 11.1%.
  6. Round to nearest integer: 11%. Select Option A.

The sum of first n odd natural numbers is:

Pattern Check~3smnemonic

  2. Sum first 2 odds (1+3) = 4, so n=2 gives 4.
  3. Match results: 1→1² and 4→2². Rule is n².
  4. Select option C: n².
More methods (2)
Visual Block~4sinvariant

See squares made of odd rows

  1. Row 1 has 1 dot (square 1x1).
  2. Row 2 adds 3 dots to make 2x2 square.
  3. Each new odd number completes the next square.
  4. Total is always n squared.
  5. Select option C: n².
Algebraic Split~5sshortcut

Separate even and odd parts of sum

  1. Sum 1 to 2n is 2n(2n+1)/2 = n(2n+1).
  2. This equals even sum (2+4+...+2n) plus odd sum.
  3. Even sum factors to 2×(1+2+...+n) = n(n+1).
  4. Subtract even from total: n(2n+1) - n(n+1) = 2n²+n - n²-n = n².
  5. Select option C: n².

The integral of (1/(x^2 + 4)) dx is:
A. arctan(x/2)/2 + CB. arctan(x) + CC. ln|x^2+4| + CD. x + C
Answer: A
Formula Match~3sshortcut

Direct a^2 coefficient check

  1. Recall standard integral of 1/(x^2+a^2) equals (1/a)arctan(x/a).
  2. Identify a^2=4, so a=2.
  3. Apply formula directly: (1/2)arctan(x/2).
  4. Add constant C and match to option A.
  5. Final answer is A
More methods (2)
Standard Sub~4smnemonic

Recognize arctan pattern

  1. Identify form 1/(u^2+a^2) with u=x, a=2.
  2. Substitute x=2t to get integral of 2/(4(t^2+1)).
  3. Factor out 2 and cancel to get integral of 1/(t^2+1).
  4. Integrate to arctan(t), then substitute back t=x/2.
  5. Multiply by the factor 1/2 from step 2.
  6. Final answer is A: arctan(x/2)/2 + C
Derivative Check~5sinvariant

Verify options by differentiation

  1. Differentiate option A: d/dx[(1/2)arctan(x/2)] = (1/2)*(1/(1+(x/2)^2))*(1/2).
  2. Simplify denominator: 1 + x^2/4 = (4+x^2)/4.
  3. Combine terms to get 1/(x^2+4), which matches the integrand.

Find the derivative of f(x) = sin(x²).
A. cos(x²)B. 2x cos(x)C. 2x cos(x²)D. x² cos(x²)
Answer: C
Pattern Recognition~2sestimate

Match standard composite function form

  1. Recognize pattern d/dx[sin(g(x))] = g'(x)cos(g(x)).
  2. Identify g(x) = x², so g'(x) = 2x.
  3. Apply formula: 2x cos(x²).
More methods (2)
Chain Rule Shortcut~3sshortcut

Outer derivative times inner derivative

  1. Identify outer function sin(u) where u = x².
  2. Derivative of sin(u) is cos(u), so keep cos(x²).
  3. Multiply by derivative of inner u=x², which is 2x.
  4. Combine to get 2x cos(x²).
Substitution Method~4smnemonic

Replace complex argument with simple variable

  1. Let u = x², making function f(u) = sin(u).
  2. Differentiate sin(u) to get cos(u).
  3. Substitute u back as x²: cos(x²).
  4. Multiply by du/dx (2x) from chain rule.
  5. Result is 2x cos(x²).

As per the provided line graph showing the monthly expenditure of a household on various items, if the expenditure on 'Food' is ₹4000 and on 'Clothing' is ₹1500, what percentage less is the expenditure on Clothing compared to Food? (Use shortcut: Calculate difference first, then apply 100 * diff/original).
A. A) 37.5%B. B) 62.5%C. C) 25%D. D) 40%
Answer: B · paper key: A

The lab answer differs from the answer key in the paper; kept for review.

Half-Check Shortcut~2sshortcut

Clothing (₹1500) is below half of Food (₹4000 = ₹2000). If it were exactly half, it would be 50% less. Since it is below half, the answer must be more than 50% less. Only option B (62.5%) is over 50%.

  1. Half of Food = 4000 / 2 = 2000
  2. Is Clothing below half? 1500 < 2000 → Yes
  3. So '% less' must be > 50%
  4. Scan options: A) 37.5% ✗ B) 62.5% ✓ C) 25% ✗ D) 40% ✗
  5. Only B is over 50% → Answer: B (62.5%)
More methods (3)
Difference-First Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use given formula: diff/original * 100

  1. Difference = 4000 - 1500 = 2500
  2. Divide by original (Food): 2500/4000 = 0.625
  3. Multiply by 100: 62.5%
Fraction Simplification~5sshortcut

Simplify ratio to fraction, subtract from 1

  1. Clothing/Food = 1500/4000 = 3/8
  2. Percentage less = (1 - 3/8) * 100 = (5/8)*100
  3. 5/8 = 0.625 = 62.5%
Complement Percentage~5sshortcut

Find Clothing as % of Food, subtract from 100%

  1. Clothing % of Food = (1500/4000)*100 = 37.5%
  2. Percentage less = 100% - 37.5% = 62.5%

Double Adjoint Power~5sshortcut

Apply determinant of adjoint formula twice

  1. Recall |adj A| = |A|^{n-1} for n×n matrix
  2. Apply again: |adj(adj A)| = |adj A|^{n-1} = (|A|^{n-1})^{n-1} = |A|^{(n-1)^2}
  3. For n=3, exponent = (3-1)^2 = 4
  4. Compute 2^4 = 16

Compute adjoint determinant sequentially

  1. First find |adj A| = |A|^{3-1} = 2^2 = 4
  2. Then |adj(adj A)| = |adj A|^{3-1} = 4^2 = 16

CTETMathematicsverified2026-11-15
What is the unit digit of the product (327)^51 × (654)^62?
A. 2B. 4C. 6D. 8
Answer: D
Cycle Method~5sshortcut

Use repeating last-digit cycles for 7 and 4

  1. Unit digit of 327 is 7; of 654 is 4.
  2. 7^51: cycle 7,9,3,1 → 51 mod 4 = 3 → unit 3.
  3. 4^62: cycle 4,6 → 62 mod 2 = 0 → unit 6.
  4. Multiply 3×6=18 → unit 8.
More methods (2)
Mod 10 Direct~5sinvariant

Compute each factor modulo 10 using congruences

  1. Compute (7^51 mod 10) × (4^62 mod 10) mod 10.
  2. 7^2=49≡-1 → 7^4≡1 → 7^51≡7^3≡343≡3.
  3. 4^2=16≡6 → 4^62≡(4^2)^31≡6^31≡6.
  4. 3×6=18≡8.
Power Pairing~6smnemonic

Break exponents into full cycles plus remainder

  1. Note 7^4 ends in 1, 4^2 ends in 6.
  2. 7^51 = (7^4)^12 × 7^3 → 1 × 343 → 3.
  3. 4^62 = (4^2)^31 → 6^31 → 6 (6^n always ends in 6).
  4. 3 × 6 = 18 → unit 8.

Use |z^n| = |z|^n

  1. |1+i| = √(1^2+1^2) = √2
  2. (√2)^8 = 2^4 = 16
More methods (2)
Repeated Squaring~5sshortcut

Square stepwise to avoid binomial

  1. (1+i)^2 = 2i
  2. (1+i)^4 = (2i)^2 = -4
  3. (1+i)^8 = (-4)^2 = 16
  4. |16| = 16
Polar Form~5smnemonic

Convert to polar, raise modulus

  1. 1+i = √2 e^{iπ/4}
  2. (√2)^8 e^{i2π} = 16·1 = 16

The equation of the circle passing through the points (0,0), (a,0) and (0,b) is:
A. x^2 + y^2 + ax + by = 0B. x^2 + y^2 - 2ax - 2by = 0C. x^2 + y^2 + 2ax + 2by = 0D. x^2 + y^2 - ax - by = 0
Answer: D
Intercept Form Mnemonic~3smnemonic

Circle through origin and axes intercepts a,b has fixed equation

  1. Recall standard result: circle through (0,0), (a,0), (0,b) is x²+y² - ax - by =0.
  2. Directly select option D.
More methods (2)
Right Triangle Diameter~5sshortcut

Right angle at origin implies hypotenuse is diameter

  1. Points (0,0), (a,0), (0,b) form a right triangle with right angle at origin.
  2. The segment joining (a,0) and (0,b) is the diameter.
  3. Center is midpoint (a/2, b/2); radius² = (a²+b²)/4.
  4. Equation: (x - a/2)² + (y - b/2)² = (a²+b²)/4.
  5. Expand and simplify to x² + y² - ax - by = 0.
General Form Substitution~8sshortcut

Plug points into x²+y²+2gx+2fy+c=0

  1. Let circle be x²+y²+2gx+2fy+c=0.
  2. Substitute (0,0) → c=0.
  3. Substitute (a,0) → a²+2ga=0 → g=-a/2.
  4. Substitute (0,b) → b²+2fb=0 → f=-b/2.
  5. Equation: x²+y² - ax - by = 0.

Let ω be a complex cube root of unity (ω ≠ 1). The value of the product (1+ω)(1+ω²)(1+ω⁴)(1+ω⁸) ... up to 2n factors is:
A. 1B. -1C. ωD. ω²
Answer: A
Direct Identity~4smnemonic

Use known product (1+ω)(1+ω²)=1 and count pairs

  1. Remember (1+ω)(1+ω²)=1
  2. Sequence 1,2,4,8,… mod 3 is 1,2,1,2,…
  3. So product has n copies of that identity
  4. Result = 1ⁿ = 1
More methods (2)
Mod 3 Pairing~5sshortcut

Exponents modulo 3 repeat 1,2; each pair multiplies to 1

  1. ω³=1 and 1+ω+ω²=0
  2. Powers 2ᵏ modulo 3 cycle: 1,2,1,2,…
  3. Factors alternate (1+ω) and (1+ω²)
  4. Pair them: (1+ω)(1+ω²)=1+ω+ω²+ω³=1
  5. 2n factors give n pairs → product = 1ⁿ = 1
Conjugate Pairing~5sinvariant

ω² is conjugate of ω; each pair gives squared magnitude 1

  1. ω² = \bar{ω} since |ω|=1
  2. (1+ω)(1+ω²)=|1+ω|²
  3. 1+ω = -ω², so |1+ω|=1
  4. Each pair equals 1
  5. 2n factors = n pairs → product = 1

Evaluate at integer points 0,1,2,3; cubic monotonic between criticals

  1. Compute f(0)=4, f(1)=2, f(2)=0, f(3)=4
  2. Since f is continuous and only one turning point at x=2
  3. Maximum among samples is 4

Factor cubic to (x-2)^2(x+1); nonnegative factors show max at endpoints

  1. Factor f(x)=x^3-3x^2+4=(x-2)^2(x+1)
  2. On [0,3], (x-2)^2≥0, x+1>0
  3. Max of (x-2)^2 is 4 at x=0,3; max of x+1 is 4 at x=3

  4. Maximum is 4

Multiply successive factors

  2. SP = CP × 1.4 × 0.75 = CP × 1.05
  3. Profit% = (1.05 - 1) × 100 = 5%

Concrete numbers avoid algebra

  1. Let Cost Price = 100
  2. Marked Price = 100 + 40% = 140
  3. Selling Price = 140 - 25% = 140×0.75 = 105
  4. Profit = 105 - 100 = 5 → Profit% = 5%

Direct profit% from markup and discount

  1. Markup = 40%, Discount = 25%
  2. Profit% = Markup - Discount - (Markup×Discount)/100
  3. Compute: 40 - 25 - (40×25)/100 = 15 - 10 = 5%

JEEMathematicsverified2026-11-18
If ω is a non-real cube root of unity, then the value of (1 - ω + ω²)(1 + ω - ω²) is:
A. -4B. 4C. 0D. -1
Answer: B
Factor as -2ω and -2ω²~3sshortcut

Directly rewrite each factor using 1+ω+ω²=0

  1. Note 1+ω+ω²=0.
  2. Then 1-ω+ω² = (1+ω+ω²) - 2ω = -2ω.
  3. And 1+ω-ω² = (1+ω+ω²) - 2ω² = -2ω².
  4. Product = (-2ω)(-2ω²) = 4ω³ = 4.
More methods (2)
Difference of Squares~4sshortcut

Rewrite as (1-(ω-ω²))(1+(ω-ω²)) = 1-(ω-ω²)²

  1. Write as (1 - (ω-ω²))(1 + (ω-ω²)) = 1 - (ω-ω²)².
  2. Expand (ω-ω²)² = ω² - 2ω³ + ω⁴ = ω² - 2 + ω.
  3. Use ω²+ω = -1 → (ω-ω²)² = -3.
  4. Product = 1 - (-3) = 4.
Sum Zero Substitution~5sshortcut

Replace ω² with -1-ω using 1+ω+ω²=0

  1. Use 1+ω+ω²=0 → ω² = -1-ω.
  2. First factor: 1-ω+ω² = 1-ω-1-ω = -2ω.
  3. Second factor: 1+ω-ω² = 1+ω+1+ω = 2+2ω = 2(1+ω).
  4. Use 1+ω = -ω² → second factor = -2ω².
  5. Multiply: (-2ω)(-2ω²)=4ω³=4.

  2. Sum counts = 15. Probability = 15/36 = 5/12

Direct count of prime sums using known dice distribution

  1. List prime sums: 2, 3, 5, 7, 11
  2. Recall ways for each sum: min(s-1, 13-s) → 1, 2, 4, 6, 2

NDAMathematicsverified2026-11-19
Evaluate: &int; e^x sin x dx
A. (e^x/2)(sin x − cos x) + CB. (e^x/2)(sin x + cos x) + CC. e^x cos x + CD. e^x sin x + C
Answer: A
Standard Formula~5smnemonic

Memorize ∫ e^{ax} sin bx dx = e^{ax}(a sin bx - b cos bx)/(a²+b²)

  1. Recall formula: ∫ e^{ax} sin(bx) dx = e^{ax}(a sin bx - b cos bx)/(a²+b²) + C
  2. Set a = 1, b = 1
  3. Compute: e^x (1·sin x - 1·cos x)/(1+1) = (e^x/2)(sin x - cos x) + C
More methods (2)
Complex Exponential~10sshortcut

Use e^{ix} to convert to simple exponential integral

  1. Write sin x = Im(e^{ix})
  2. Integrand becomes Im(e^x e^{ix}) = Im(e^{(1+i)x})
  3. Integrate: ∫ e^{(1+i)x} dx = e^{(1+i)x}/(1+i) + C
  4. Multiply numerator and denominator by (1-i): e^{(1+i)x}(1-i)/2
  5. Take imaginary part: (e^x/2)(sin x - cos x) + C
Double Parts~15sshortcut

Integrate by parts twice and solve for I

  1. Let I = ∫ e^x sin x dx
  2. First parts: u=sin x, dv=e^x dx → I = e^x sin x - ∫ e^x cos x dx
  3. Second parts: ∫ e^x cos x dx = e^x cos x + ∫ e^x sin x dx = e^x cos x + I
  4. Substitute: I = e^x sin x - e^x cos x - I → 2I = e^x(sin x - cos x) → I = (e^x/2)(sin x - cos x) + C

use (1-cos x)/x^2 = 1/2

More methods (2)
L'Hopital's Rule~5sshortcut

differentiate numerator and denominator

  1. Differentiate numerator: -sin x
  2. Differentiate denominator: 1
  3. Evaluate at x=0: -sin 0 = 0
Series Expansion~5sshortcut

use Maclaurin series for cos x

  1. cos x = 1 - x^2/2 + O(x^4)
  2. Substitute: (1 - x^2/2 - 1)/x = -x/2 + O(x^3)
  3. Limit as x→0 is 0

Distance between the parallel lines 2x + 3y = 6 and 4x + 6y = 11 is:
A. 1/√13B. √13/26C. 2/√13D. 5/√13
Answer: B
Coeff halve shortcut~5sshortcut

Recognize second line is double first, halve it then use simple distance formula

  1. Divide 4x+6y=11 by 2 → 2x+3y=11/2
  2. Compute Δc = |6‑11/2| = 1/2
  3. Compute norm = √(2^2+3^2)=√13
  4. Distance = Δc / norm = (1/2)/√13 = √13/26
More methods (2)
Invariant c‑difference~5sinvariant

For parallel lines ax+by=c, distance = |c2‑c1|/√(a^2+b^2) directly

  1. Write both lines as ax+by=c: first is 2x+3y=6, second divide by2 → 2x+3y=11/2
  2. Δc = |6‑11/2| = 1/2
  3. Denominator √(2^2+3^2)=√13
  4. Distance = (1/2)/√13 = √13/26
Point‑line distance~6sshortcut

Pick an easy point on one line, then use point‑to‑line formula on the other

  1. Set x=0 in 2x+3y=6 → point P(0,2)
  2. Use formula |4·0+6·2‑11|/√(4^2+6^2)
  3. Compute numerator |12‑11|=1, denominator √52=2√13
  4. Distance = 1/(2√13)=√13/26

Successive Discount Formula~4smnemonic

Use a+b−ab/100 for two successive discounts

  1. Add the two discounts: 10 + 5 = 15
  2. Subtract product divided by 100: (10×5)/100 = 0.5
  3. Result: 15 − 0.5 = 14.5%

Multiply remaining fractions to get final price

  1. Convert discounts to remaining fractions: 10% off → 0.90, 5% off → 0.95
  2. Multiply: 0.90 × 0.95 = 0.855
  3. Subtract from 1: 1 − 0.855 = 0.145 = 14.5%

  1. First discount 10% leaves 90% of original
  2. Second discount 5% of 90% = 4.5%
  3. Total discount = 10% + 4.5% = 14.5%

A tea vendor mixes 3 kg of Type A tea costing Rs 120 per kg with 2 kg of Type B tea costing Rs 150 per kg. He sells half of this mixture at a 10 % profit on its cost. He then adds 1 kg of Type C tea costing Rs 180 per kg to the remaining mixture and sells the rest at the same selling price per kg as used for the first half. What is the overall profit percentage on the whole transaction (rounded to two decimal places)?
A. A. 10.00%B. B. 5.00%C. C. 3.71%D. D. 7.50%
Answer: C
Average Cost vs SP~15sshortcut

Compare selling price per kg to average cost per kg of all tea purchased

  1. Total cost = 3×120 + 2×150 + 1×180 = 840; total kg = 6 → avg cost = 140
  2. First half cost = (3×120+2×150)/2 = 330; half qty = 2.5 kg
  3. SP per kg = 330×1.1 / 2.5 = 145.2
  4. Profit% = (145.2−140)/140 × 100 = 3.71% → Option C
More methods (2)
Total Revenue Minus Cost~15sshortcut

Find constant selling price per kg from first half, multiply by total kg sold (6), subtract total cost

  1. Total cost = 3×120 + 2×150 + 1×180 = 840
  2. First half cost = (3×120+2×150)/2 = 330; half qty = 2.5 kg
  3. SP per kg = 330×1.1 / 2.5 = 145.2
  4. Total revenue = 145.2 × 6 = 871.2
  5. Profit% = (871.2−840)/840 × 100 = 3.71% → Option C
Profit/Loss Split~20sshortcut

Compute 10% profit on first half, then loss on second half sold at same SP, sum for net profit

  1. First half cost = (3×120+2×150)/2 = 330; profit = 10% of 330 = 33
  2. Second half cost = 330 (remaining) + 180 = 510; qty = 3.5 kg
  3. SP per kg = 330×1.1/2.5 = 145.2; revenue = 3.5×145.2 = 508.2
  4. Loss on second = 510−508.2 = 1.8; net profit = 33−1.8 = 31.2
  5. Total cost = 840; profit% = 31.2/840×100 = 3.71% → Option C

Rahul invests ₹20,000 in a scheme offering 8% p.a. compound interest, compounded annually. At the end of the first year, the bank revises the rate to 10% p.a. for the second year. Rahul withdraws 25% of the amount (including interest) at the end of the first year and reinvests the remaining at the revised rate for the second year. What is the total amount he receives at the end of the second year?
A. ₹23,220B. ₹22,896C. ₹23,760D. ₹21,600
Answer: A
Combined Growth Factor~5sshortcut

Merge all multipliers into a single factor

  1. Effective factor = 1.08 × (0.25 + 0.75 × 1.10) = 1.08 × (0.25 + 0.825) = 1.08 × 1.075.
  2. Compute 1.08 × 1.075 = 1.161.
  3. Final amount = 20000 × 1.161 = 23220.
More methods (2)
Stepwise Decimal~8sshortcut

Compute year-by-year with simple decimals

  1. First year amount: 20000 × 1.08 = 21600.
  2. Withdrawal (25%): 21600 × 0.25 = 5400.
  3. Reinvested principal: 21600 - 5400 = 16200.
  4. Second year amount: 16200 × 1.10 = 17820.
  5. Total received: 5400 + 17820 = 23220.

Use fractions to avoid decimals and combine steps

  1. Convert rates to fractions: 8% = 27/25, 10% = 11/10, 25% withdrawal = 1/4 kept = 3/4.
  2. Total factor = (27/25) * (1/4 + 3/4 * 11/10) = (27/25) * (1/4 + 33/40) = (27/25) * (43/40).
  3. Multiply: 20000 * 27 * 43 / 1000 = 20 * 27 * 43 = 23220.

NDAMathematicsneeds review2026-11-20
Machine A produces 120 units in 4 hrs, Machine B 150 units in 5 hrs and Machine C 180 units in 6 hrs. Each machine works continuously for 2 hrs and then undergoes a 30‑minute maintenance break, after which it resumes the same pattern. All three machines start together at 9:00 AM. When will the total cumulative production first reach or exceed 1000 units?
A. A) 10:36 PMB. B) 8:06 PMC. C) 9:45 PMD. D) 11:15 PM
Answer: A

The Scientist and Verifier disagreed; this method is kept for review, not published as verified.

Synchronized Cycle Counting~10sshortcut

All machines share 30 u/hr rate and 2.5 h cycle, so treat as one 90 u/hr machine with periodic breaks.

  1. Per-cycle output: 3 × 30 u/hr × 2 h = 180 units per 2.5 h cycle.
  2. Full cycles for 1000: 5 cycles give 900 units in 12.5 h (9:00 AM → 9:30 PM).
  3. Remaining 100 units at 90 u/hr = 1 h 6.67 min.
  4. Add to 9:30 PM → 10:36:40 PM, first exceed at 10:36 PM.
More methods (2)
Work-Period Accumulation~12sshortcut

Breaks are synchronized; count only 2-hour work blocks each yielding 180 units.

  1. Each 2-hour work block: 3 machines × 30 u/hr × 2 h = 180 units.
  2. 1000 ÷ 180 = 5 full blocks (900 units) + 100 units remainder.
  3. 5 blocks span 5 × (2 h + 0.5 h) = 12.5 h → 9:30 PM.
  4. Next block starts 9:30 PM; 100 units need 100/90 h = 1 h 6.67 min.
  5. Reach 1000 at 10:36:40 PM → 10:36 PM.
Rate-Uniformity Shortcut~15smnemonic

Identical hourly rates (30) and schedules allow simple cycle arithmetic.

  1. Confirm rates: A=30, B=30, C=30 u/hr.
  2. Cycle = 2 h work + 0.5 h break = 2.5 h; output = 90 × 2 = 180.
  3. 1000 = 5×180 + 100 → 5 cycles (12.5 h) to 9:30 PM, 900 units.
  4. Extra time = 100/90 h = 1 1/9 h = 1 h 6 min 40 s.
  5. 9:30 PM + 1 h 6 min 40 s = 10:36:40 PM → 10:36 PM.

NDAMathematicsverified2026-11-20
A retailer marks up a commodity by 25% above its cost price. He then offers a discount of x% on this marked price. If the final selling price equals the original cost price plus half of the initial profit he would have made had there been no discount, what is the value of x?
A. 10B. 12C. 15D. 20
Answer: A
Percentage Difference~5sshortcut

Compute discount directly from percentages

  1. Cost = 100% → Marked = 125%
  2. Initial profit = 25% → Half profit = 12.5%
  3. Final price = 100% + 12.5% = 112.5%
  4. Discount amount = 125% - 112.5% = 12.5%
  5. Discount % = (12.5/125)×100 = 10%
More methods (2)
Fraction Method~8sshortcut

Use fractions to avoid decimals

  1. Cost = C
  2. Markup 25% = 1/4 → Marked = 5/4 C
  3. Profit = 1/4 C → Half profit = 1/8 C
  4. Final price = C + 1/8 C = 9/8 C
  5. Discount: (5/4)(1 - x/100) = 9/8
  6. Solve: 1 - x/100 = 9/10 → x = 10
Algebraic Equation~10sshortcut

Set up equation with cost C

  1. Let cost = C
  2. Marked price = 1.25C
  3. Initial profit = 0.25C
  4. Final price = C + 0.125C = 1.125C
  5. Discounted price = 1.25C(1 - x/100)
  6. Solve 1.25(1 - x/100) = 1.125 → x = 10

What is the unit digit of 7^{2023}?
A. 7B. 9C. 3D. 1
Answer: C

Memorize 7^n unit digits for n=1,2,3,4; use remainder

  1. Cycle: 7¹=7, 7²=9, 7³=3, 7⁴=1
  2. 2023 mod 4 = 3 → pick 3rd entry: 3
More methods (2)
Cycle of 4~3sinvariant

Powers of 7 repeat unit digits every 4: 7,9,3,1

  1. 2023 ÷ 4 = 505 remainder 3
  2. 3rd position in cycle 7→9→3→1 is 3

  1. 7^4 ≡ 1 (mod 10)
  2. 2023 = 4×505 + 3
  3. 7^2023 ≡ 7^3 ≡ 343 ≡ 3 (mod 10)

Reduce exponents modulo 4 using Euler's theorem for mod 10

  2. 7^2023 = 7^(4*505+3) ≡ 7^3 ≡ 343 ≡ 3 (mod 10)

  3. 3^n units digit cycle: 3,9,7,1 repeats every 4

  5. Sum units digits: 3+1=4 → answer D

Align both cycles and pick the correct positions

  1. 7^n cycle (n=1..4): 7,9,3,1
  2. 3^n cycle (n=1..4): 3,9,7,1
  3. 2023 mod 4 = 3 → 7^2023 ends with 3

Which of the following fractions is the greatest?
A. 3/5B. 5/7C. 7/9D. 9/11
Answer: D
Denominator Trend~5sinvariant

Fractions equal 1 minus 2 over denominator; larger denominator means larger value.

  1. Each fraction is (denom-2)/denom = 1 - 2/denom.
  2. As denominator increases, 2/denom decreases, so fraction increases.
  3. Largest denominator is 11, so 9/11 is greatest.
More methods (2)
Complement Comparison~5sshortcut

Compare complements 1 - fraction = 2/denom; smallest complement gives largest fraction.

  1. Complements: 2/5, 2/7, 2/9, 2/11.
  2. Smallest complement is 2/11, so 9/11 is largest.
Quick Decimal~8sestimate

Mentally approximate decimals; 9/11 ≈ 0.818 exceeds others.

  1. 3/5=0.6; 5/7≈0.714; 7/9≈0.778; 9/11≈0.818.
  2. 0.818 is highest, so 9/11.

NDAMathematicsverified2026-11-22
The value of the definite integral ∫_0^(π/2) log(tan x) dx is:
A. π/2B. 0C. 1D. -1
Answer: B
Symmetry Substitution~5sshortcut

Exploit tan(π/2 - x) = cot x = 1/tan x

  1. Let I = ∫_0^{π/2} log(tan x) dx.
  2. Substitute x → π/2 - x: I = ∫_0^{π/2} log(tan(π/2 - x)) dx = ∫_0^{π/2} log(cot x) dx = ∫_0^{π/2} -log(tan x) dx = -I.
  3. Thus I = -I ⇒ 2I = 0 ⇒ I = 0.
More methods (2)
Log Difference~10sshortcut

log(tan x) = log(sin x) - log(cos x); known integrals cancel

  1. Recall ∫_0^{π/2} log(sin x) dx = ∫_0^{π/2} log(cos x) dx = -π/2 log 2.
  2. Write log(tan x) = log(sin x) - log(cos x).
  3. Integral = (-π/2 log 2) - (-π/2 log 2) = 0.
Reciprocal Substitution~10sshortcut

Let t = tan x, then t → 1/t shows integral is zero

  1. Substitute t = tan x, dx = dt/(1+t^2): I = ∫_0^∞ log(t)/(1+t^2) dt.
  2. Split at 1: I = ∫_0^1 + ∫_1^∞. In second, let u = 1/t ⇒ du = -dt/t^2.
  3. ∫_1^∞ log(t)/(1+t^2) dt = ∫_1^0 log(1/u)/(1+1/u^2) (-du/u^2) = ∫_0^1 -log(u)/(1+u^2) du.
  4. Sum cancels: I = 0.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
APFCMathematicsverified2026-11-22
In how many ways can 5 different books be arranged on a shelf such that two particular books are always together?
A. 24B. 72C. 48D. 120
Answer: C
Bundle & Permute~5sshortcut

Treat the two books as a single block, permute blocks, then permute inside.

  1. Consider the two particular books as one combined item.
  2. Now we have 4 items to arrange: the block and the other 3 books.
  3. Arrange these 4 items in 4! = 24 ways.
  4. The two books inside the block can be ordered in 2! = 2 ways.
  5. Multiply: 24 × 2 = 48.
More methods (2)
Gap Insertion~10sshortcut

Arrange the other books first, then place the pair in gaps.

  1. Arrange the 3 other books in 3! = 6 ways.
  2. These create 4 gaps (including ends) to insert the pair as a block.
  3. Choose 1 gap out of 4: 4 ways.
  4. The two books can be ordered within the block in 2! = 2 ways.
  5. Total: 6 × 4 × 2 = 48.
Total Minus Separated~15sestimate

Subtract arrangements where the two books are apart from total arrangements.

  1. Total arrangements of 5 books: 5! = 120.
  2. Count arrangements where the two books are separated: treat them as distinct and place them in non-adjacent positions.
  3. Alternatively, use complement: total - together = separated, but we want together.
  4. Directly: together = total - separated. Separated count: arrange 3 others (3! = 6), choose 2 gaps out of 4 for the two books (P(4,2)=12), total separated = 6×12=72.
  5. Together = 120 - 72 = 48.

The sum of the first 10 terms of an Arithmetic Progression is 100 and the sum of the first 20 terms is 400. What is the sum of the first 30 terms?
A. 600B. 700C. 800D. 900
Answer: D
Block Sum AP~5sshortcut

Sums of equal-length blocks form an AP

  1. S₁₀ = 100, S₂₀ = 400
  2. First block sum = 100
  3. Second block sum = 300
  4. Common difference = 200
  5. Third block sum = 500
  6. S₃₀ = 400 + 500 = 900
More methods (2)
Quadratic Fit~10sshortcut

Sₙ is quadratic with no constant term

  1. Let Sₙ = An² + Bn
  2. 100A + 10B = 100
  3. 400A + 20B = 400
  4. Simplify: 10A + B = 10, 20A + B = 20
  5. Subtract: 10A = 10 → A = 1, B = 0
  6. Sₙ = n² → S₃₀ = 900
Standard Formula~15sinvariant

Solve for a and d directly

  1. S₁₀ = 5(2a+9d) = 100 → 2a+9d=20
  2. S₂₀ = 10(2a+19d) = 400 → 2a+19d=40
  3. Subtract: 10d = 20 → d = 2
  4. Back-substitute: 2a = 20 - 18 = 2 → a = 1
  5. S₃₀ = 15(2a+29d) = 15(2+58) = 900

If α and β are the roots of the quadratic equation 2x² - 5x + 3 = 0, what is the value of α² + β²?
A. 17/4B. 13/4C. 19/4D. 21/4
Answer: B
Discriminant Shortcut~4smnemonic

Use formula (b²-2ac)/a² for sum of squares

  1. Formula: α²+β² = (b²-2ac)/a²
  2. Plug a=2,b=-5,c=3: (25-12)/4 = 13/4
More methods (2)
Vieta's Identity~5sshortcut

Use sum and product of roots to compute sum of squares

  1. α+β = 5/2, αβ = 3/2
  2. α²+β² = (α+β)² - 2αβ
  3. = (5/2)² - 2*(3/2) = 25/4 - 3 = 13/4
Root Substitution~7sshortcut

Express α² and β² from the equation and sum

  1. 2α² -5α+3=0 ⇒ α² = (5α-3)/2
  2. Similarly β² = (5β-3)/2
  3. α²+β² = (5(α+β)-6)/2
  4. = (5*(5/2)-6)/2 = (25/2-6)/2 = (13/2)/2 = 13/4

  3. 50 is even → 4^50 ends in 6
  4. 6+6=12 → unit digit 2

Use known unit digit cycles for 6 and 4

  3. 50 is even → 4^50 ends in 6
  4. Add unit digits: 6+6=12

CDSMathematicsverified2026-11-23
If tanθ = 1, then secθ + cosecθ = ?
A. √2B. 2√2C. 3√2D. 2
Answer: B
Pythagorean Shortcut~4sshortcut

Use 1+tan²=sec² and 1+cot²=cosec²

  1. 1+tan²θ = sec²θ ⇒ secθ = √(1+1) = √2
  2. cotθ = 1/tanθ = 1 ⇒ 1+cot²θ = cosec²θ ⇒ cosecθ = √2
  3. secθ + cosecθ = √2 + √2 = 2√2
More methods (2)
Direct Angle Substitution~5sshortcut

tanθ=1 gives θ=45°, use known values

  1. tanθ=1 ⇒ θ=45° (or π/4)
  2. sin45°=cos45°=√2/2
  3. secθ=1/cosθ=√2, cosecθ=1/sinθ=√2
  4. Sum = √2+√2 = 2√2
Identity Ratio~5sshortcut

Express sum as (sin+cos)/(sin cos), use tan=1

  1. secθ+cosecθ = (sinθ+cosθ)/(sinθ cosθ)
  2. tanθ=1 ⇒ sinθ=cosθ
  3. Let sinθ=cosθ=k, then k^2+k^2=1 ⇒ k=±√2/2
  4. Sum = (2k)/(k^2) = 2/k = ±2√2, take positive 2√2

CDSMathematicsverified2026-11-23
A contractor undertakes a project to be completed in 20 days. He starts with 20 men and 30 women. The efficiency of a man is twice that of a woman. After 10 days, 5 men leave and 10 women join. After another 5 days, 5 men join and 5 women leave. The work finishes exactly on the 20th day. The total wages paid to all workers is ₹1,42,500, distributed in proportion to the work done by each worker. What is the daily wage of a woman?
A. ₹80B. ₹100C. ₹120D. ₹150
Answer: B
Efficiency Wage Shortcut~5sshortcut

Convert work to unit totals using efficiency, then use total wage = unit wage × total units

  1. Set woman's efficiency =1, man's =2.
  2. Compute work each period: 10 days·(20·2+30·1)=700; 5 days·(15·2+40·1)=350; 5 days·(20·2+35·1)=375.
  3. Add: total units = 700+350+375 = 1425.
  4. Total wages = ₹1,42,500 = unit wage × 1425 ⇒ unit wage = 100.
  5. Woman's daily wage = unit wage = ₹100.
More methods (2)
Weighted Man‑Day~6sshortcut

Treat a man as two women, sum weighted man‑days, then divide total wages

  1. Convert men to women: 20 men = 40 women; add 30 women → 70 women for first 10 days.
  2. Work = 70·10 = 700 woman‑days.
  3. Next 5 days: 15 men =30 women +40 women =70 → 70·5 =350.
  4. Last 5 days: 20 men =40 women +35 women =75 → 75·5 =375.
  5. Total woman‑day equivalents = 1425; wage per woman‑day = 1,42,500/1425 =100.
  6. Daily wage of a woman = ₹100.
Proportional Pay~7smnemonic

Use proportion of work each worker does; solve for woman's rate directly

  1. Let woman’s daily wage = w, man’s =2w.
  2. Total wage = w·(30·20 +20·2·20) = w·(600+800)=w·1400? Wait compute correctly:

A cricket team needs to chase a target of 250 runs in 50 overs. In the first 30 overs, they score at a run rate of 4.5 runs per over. What should be the required run rate in the remaining overs to reach the target?
A. 5.5B. 5.75C. 6.0D. 6.25
Answer: B
Deficit per Over~8sshortcut

Find total deficit from target run rate and spread over remaining overs

  1. Target run rate = 250 / 50 = 5
  2. Deficit per over = 5 - 4.5 = 0.5
  3. Total deficit = 0.5 * 30 = 15
  4. Extra per remaining over = 15 / 20 = 0.75
  5. Required rate = 5 + 0.75 = 5.75
More methods (2)
Direct Calculation~10sshortcut

Compute runs scored, remaining runs, divide by remaining overs

  1. Runs scored = 30 * 4.5 = 135
  2. Remaining runs = 250 - 135 = 115
  3. Remaining overs = 50 - 30 = 20
  4. Required rate = 115 / 20 = 5.75
Weighted Average~10sshortcut

Set up average equation and solve for unknown run rate

  1. Equation: (30*4.5 + 20*x) / 50 = 5
  2. 135 + 20x = 250
  3. 20x = 115
  4. x = 5.75

A and B together can complete a task in 10 days. They work together for 4 days, after which A leaves. B finishes the remaining work in 12 days. If A's efficiency were 25% higher, how many days would A alone take to complete the entire task?
A. 16 daysB. 18 daysC. 20 daysD. 24 days
Answer: A
Rate Subtraction~5sshortcut

Direct rates from given times

  1. Combined rate = 1/10 task/day
  2. Work in 4 days = 4/10 = 2/5; remaining = 3/5
  3. B's rate = (3/5)/12 = 1/20 task/day
  4. A's rate = 1/10 - 1/20 = 1/20 task/day
  5. 25% higher rate = 1.25 * 1/20 = 1/16 task/day
  6. Time = 16 days
More methods (2)
Efficiency Ratio~5sinvariant

Solve for individual days then scale

  1. Let A take x days, B take y days: 1/x + 1/y = 1/10
  2. 4 days together complete 4/10 = 2/5; remaining 3/5 done by B in 12 days => 12/y = 3/5 => y = 20
  3. Then 1/x = 1/10 - 1/20 = 1/20 => x = 20
  4. 25% higher efficiency means 1.25 times rate => days = 20/1.25 = 16
Work Units~6smnemonic

Use LCM to avoid fractions

  1. Total work = LCM(10,12,4) = 60 units
  2. Combined rate = 60/10 = 6 units/day
  3. 4 days together = 24 units; remaining = 36 units
  4. B's rate = 36/12 = 3 units/day
  5. A's rate = 6 - 3 = 3 units/day
  6. 25% higher = 3.75 units/day; time = 60/3.75 = 16 days

A retailer marks up a commodity by 25% above its cost price. He then offers a discount of x% on this marked price. If the final selling price equals the original cost price plus half of the initial profit he would have made had there been no discount, what is the value of x?
A. 10B. 12C. 15D. 20
Answer: A
Ratio Insight~4sinvariant

Directly compare final and marked prices as decimals

  1. Final price = Cost + 0.5*(0.25 Cost) = 1.125 Cost
  2. Marked price = 1.25 Cost
  3. Ratio final/marked = 1.125 / 1.25 = 0.9
  4. Discount = 1 - 0.9 = 0.1 = 10%
More methods (2)
Assume Cost 100~5sshortcut

Plug in 100 for cost to avoid algebra

  1. Let cost = 100
  2. Marked price = 125 (25% markup)
  3. Profit = 25, half profit = 12.5
  4. Final price = 100 + 12.5 = 112.5
  5. Discount % = (125 - 112.5) / 125 * 100 = 10%
Fraction Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use fractions for exact mental arithmetic

  1. Cost = C, 25% markup = 1/4 C
  2. Marked price = 5/4 C
  3. Initial profit = 1/4 C, half = 1/8 C
  4. Final price = C + 1/8 C = 9/8 C
  5. Discount factor = (9/8) / (5/4) = 9/10, so discount = 10%

CDSMathematicsverified2026-11-23
Four workers, each of whom can complete a piece of work alone in 20 days, start the job together. After 4 days, two of these workers leave and are replaced by three new workers, each of whom can complete the same work alone in 30 days. Assuming all workers work at the same constant rate, how many additional days will be required to finish the remaining work?
A. A. 1 dayB. B. 2 daysC. C. 3 daysD. D. 4 days
Answer: A
Rate Equality~5sinvariant

Notice new team's combined rate equals original team's rate.

  1. Original 4 workers rate = 4/20 = 1/5 work/day.
  2. After 4 days, work done = 4*(1/5) = 4/5; remaining = 1/5.
  3. New team: 2 original (2/20=1/10) + 3 new (3/30=1/10) = 1/5 work/day.
  4. Same rate → remaining work takes 1 day.
More methods (2)
Fraction Done~7sshortcut

Compute fraction completed, then find new rate as fraction per day.

  1. Each original worker: 1/20 per day. 4 workers: 1/5 per day.
  2. In 4 days: 4/5 done, 1/5 left.
  3. Two original remain: 2/20 = 1/10 per day.
  4. Three new: 3/30 = 1/10 per day.
  5. Combined new rate = 1/10+1/10 = 1/5 per day.
  6. Time = (1/5) / (1/5) = 1 day.
LCM Units~10sshortcut

Use LCM of 20 and 30 as total work units for integer arithmetic.

  1. Let total work = LCM(20,30) = 60 units.
  2. Original worker rate = 60/20 = 3 units/day.
  3. 4 workers do 12 units/day; in 4 days = 48 units.
  4. Remaining = 12 units.
  5. New team: 2 original = 6 units/day, 3 new = 60/30=2 each → 6 units/day, total 12 units/day.
  6. Time = 12/12 = 1 day.

After the discounts, a service charge of 5% is levied on the resulting price. If the customer finally pays ₹1134, what is the cost price of the article?

  1. Multiply successive factors: 1.5 × 0.8 × 0.9 × 1.05 = 1.134
  2. Divide final price by multiplier: 1134 ÷ 1.134 = 1000

Fraction Shortcut~6smnemonic

Use fractions to see 1.134 = 1134/1000 instantly

  1. Write factors as fractions: 3/2, 4/5, 9/10, 21/20
  2. Multiply: (3×4×9×21)/(2×5×10×20) = 2268/2000 = 1.134

Combine discounts into one then apply GST

A fair coin is tossed 4 times. The probability of getting at least one head is:
A. 1/16B. 7/8C. 3/4D. 15/16
Answer: D
Complement Rule~5sshortcut

subtract all-tails probability from 1

  1. Probability of all tails = (1/2)^4 = 1/16
  2. P(at least one head) = 1 - 1/16 = 15/16
More methods (2)
Binary Pattern~5sinvariant

treat tosses as bits; exclude all-zero pattern

  1. Each toss is a bit: H=1, T=0
  2. At least one head means not all zeros
  3. All-zero pattern occurs once in 16
  4. Probability = 15/16
Direct Count~6smnemonic

count total outcomes minus the single all-tails case

  1. Total outcomes = 2^4 = 16
  2. Only one outcome has no heads (TTTT)
  3. Favorable outcomes = 16 - 1 = 15
  4. Probability = 15/16

NDAMathematicsverified2026-11-24
If f(x) = x³ + 2x² - 5x + 7, what is f'(1)?
A. 0B. 1C. 3D. 2
Answer: D
Coefficient-Exponent Sum~4sshortcut

f'(1) equals sum of exponent times coefficient

  1. Write terms with exponents: 1x³, 2x², -5x¹, 7x⁰
  2. Multiply each coefficient by its exponent: 3, 4, -5, 0
  3. Add: 3+4-5=2
More methods (2)
Power Rule~5sshortcut

Differentiate termwise then substitute

  1. Differentiate: f'(x)=3x²+4x-5
  2. Evaluate at x=1: 3+4-5=2
Horner's Scheme~6sshortcut

Evaluate polynomial and derivative together

  1. Horner for f at 1: coeffs 1,2,-5,7 → f(1)=5
  2. Derivative via Horner: bring down 1, then 1*1+2=3, 3*1+(-5)=-2, -2*1+7=5
  3. Derivative coefficients: 1, 1*1+3=4, 4*1+(-2)=2 → f'(1)=2

What is the unit digit of (7^95 + 3^58)?
A. 0B. 2C. 4D. 6
Answer: B
Modular Arithmetic~4sinvariant

Compute each term modulo 10 using Euler's theorem

  1. 7^4 ≡ 1 (mod 10) → 7^95 = 7^(4·23+3) ≡ 7^3 ≡ 343 ≡ 3 (mod 10)
  2. 3^4 ≡ 1 (mod 10) → 3^58 = 3^(4·14+2) ≡ 3^2 ≡ 9 (mod 10)
  3. Sum mod 10: 3 + 9 = 12 ≡ 2 (mod 10)
  4. Answer B
More methods (2)
Cycle Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use 4-length cycles for unit digits

  1. 7^n unit digits cycle: 7,9,3,1 (period 4)
  2. 95 mod 4 = 3 → 7^95 unit digit = 3
  3. 3^n unit digits cycle: 3,9,7,1 (period 4)
  4. 58 mod 4 = 2 → 3^58 unit digit = 9
  5. Add: 3 + 9 = 12 → unit digit 2
  6. Answer B
Pattern Memory~5smnemonic

Recall memorized cycles for 7 and 3

  1. Memorize: 7^n ends in 7,9,3,1 repeating; 3^n ends in 3,9,7,1 repeating
  2. 95 ÷ 4 remainder 3 → 7^95 ends in 3
  3. 58 ÷ 4 remainder 2 → 3^58 ends in 9
  4. 3 + 9 = 12 → unit digit 2
  5. Answer B

NDAMathematicsverified2026-11-24
The value of the definite integral ∫₀^(π/2) sin³x / (sin³x + cos³x) dx is:
A. π/4B. π/2C. π/8D. 0
Answer: A
Known Formula Recall~3smnemonic

Memorize that ∫₀^(π/2) sin^n x/(sin^n x+cos^n x) dx = π/4 for any n.

  1. Recognize the integral matches the standard form with n=3.
  2. Apply the known result: value = π/4.
More methods (2)
Complementary Sum~4sshortcut

Note that f(x)+f(π/2-x)=1, so integral = half the interval length.

  1. Define f(x)=sin³x/(sin³x+cos³x).
  2. Observe f(π/2-x)=cos³x/(cos³x+sin³x).
  3. Then f(x)+f(π/2-x)=1.
  4. ∫₀^(π/2) f(x) dx = 1/2 ∫₀^(π/2) [f(x)+f(π/2-x)] dx = 1/2 * π/2 = π/4.
Symmetry Trick~5sshortcut

Use substitution x → π/2 - x to get a second integral, add them to simplify.

  1. Let I = ∫₀^(π/2) sin³x/(sin³x+cos³x) dx.
  2. Substitute x = π/2 - t → I = ∫₀^(π/2) cos³t/(cos³t+sin³t) dt.
  3. Add the two expressions: 2I = ∫₀^(π/2) (sin³x+cos³x)/(sin³x+cos³x) dx = ∫₀^(π/2) 1 dx = π/2.
  4. Therefore I = π/4.

Mixture & Dilution 1

INVENTEDMixture & Dilutionverified2026-11-24
A merchant has 500 L of a solution containing 8% salt. He removes 50 L of the mixture and replaces it with water. Then he adds 30 L of a 20% salt solution. After that, he removes 40 L of the resulting mixture and again replaces it with water. What is the final percentage of salt in the solution (rounded to two decimal places)?
A. 7.33%B. 7.50%C. 7.20%D. 7.00%
Answer: 7.33%
Carry salt mass, not %~25salternative
  1. Track only the mass of salt; every removal takes salt in proportion to the volume removed.
  2. Start 40 kg salt in 500 L. Remove 50 L -> salt x (1 - 50/500) = 40 x 0.9 = 36 kg, then top up to 500 L.
  3. Add 30 L of 20% (6 kg) -> 42 kg in 530 L.
  4. Remove 40 L -> 42 x (1 - 40/530) = 42 x 490/530 = 38.83 kg in 530 L after topping up.
  5. Final % = 38.83 / 530 x 100 = 7.33%. Never recompute the concentration mid-way; one salt column avoids every rounding slip.

The rule is: if the first file selected is 'Pending', the second MUST be 'Closed'; if the first is 'Closed', the second can be anything. If the officer picks a 'Pending' file first, what is the probability that the SECOND file selected turns out to be 'Pending' as well?
01/43/85/12
Answer: 0
Rule-Based Deduction Method~10salternative
  1. Identify the rule: if first file is Pending, second must be Closed.
  2. Given first is Pending, the rule forces second to be Closed.
  3. Thus probability second is Pending is 0.

A retailer marks up a commodity by 25% above its cost price. He then offers a discount of x% on this marked price. If the final selling price equals the original cost price plus half of the initial profit he would have made had there been no discount, what is the value of x?
10121520
Answer: 10

  1. Take cost price as 8 (since 25% = 1/4). Marked price = 8 + 2 = 10.
  2. Initial profit = 2, half profit = 1. Selling price = 8 + 1 = 9.
  3. Discount = 10 - 9 = 1. Discount% = (1/10)×100 = 10%.

11%12%13%14%
Answer: 11%

  3. Profit % on selling price = ((0.99 - 0.88) / 0.99) × 100 = 11.11% ≈ 11%

Pipe A can fill a tank in 12 hours and Pipe B can empty the full tank in 18 hours. If both pipes are opened simultaneously, in how many hours will the empty tank be filled?
A. 30 hoursB. 36 hoursC. 24 hoursD. 42 hours
Answer: B
Product/Difference~5sshortcut

Direct formula for one fill one empty pipe

  1. Multiply fill and empty times: 12 × 18 = 216
  2. Subtract fill from empty: 18 - 12 = 6
  3. Divide product by difference: 216 / 6 = 36
More methods (2)
Rate Subtraction~8smnemonic

Subtract rates using common denominator 36

  1. Common denominator of 12 and 18 is 36
  2. Fill rate = 1/12 = 3/36 tank per hour
  3. Empty rate = 1/18 = 2/36 tank per hour
  4. Net rate = 1/36 tank per hour → 36 hours
LCM Units~10sshortcut

Use LCM as tank capacity to avoid fractions

  1. LCM of 12 and 18 = 36 (tank capacity)
  2. Pipe A fills 36/12 = 3 units per hour
  3. Pipe B empties 36/18 = 2 units per hour
  4. Net fill = 3 - 2 = 1 unit per hour
  5. Time = 36 / 1 = 36 hours

IBPS-POQuantitative Aptitudeverified2026-11-16
A and B together can complete a work in 12 days. B and C together can complete the same work in 15 days. C and A together can complete it in 20 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?
A. 30 daysB. 24 daysC. 20 daysD. 18 days
Answer: A
Formula Shortcut~5sinvariant

Direct formula for A from three pairwise times

  1. Use A = 2 / (1/12 + 1/20 - 1/15)
  2. Compute 1/12+1/20 = 2/15
  3. 2/15 - 1/15 = 1/15
  4. 2 ÷ (1/15) = 30
More methods (2)
LCM & Sum~10sshortcut

Use LCM as total work, sum pairwise rates to get individual

  1. Take LCM of 12,15,20 = 60 as total work
  2. Daily rates: A+B=5, B+C=4, C+A=3
  3. Add all three: 2(A+B+C)=12 → A+B+C=6
  4. Subtract B+C: A = 6-4 = 2 units/day
  5. Days = 60/2 = 30
Pairwise Subtraction~10smnemonic

Add reciprocal equations, isolate 1/A

  1. Write rates: 1/A+1/B=1/12, 1/B+1/C=1/15, 1/C+1/A=1/20
  2. Add all: 2(1/A+1/B+1/C)=1/12+1/15+1/20=1/5
  3. So 1/A+1/B+1/C=1/10
  4. Subtract 1/B+1/C=1/15 → 1/A=1/10-1/15=1/30
  5. A = 30 days

  3. Net change = 44 + (-25) + (44×-25)/100 = 19 - 11 = 8% increase

Direct application of PT² = PA·PB

  2. Divide by PA: 144 ÷ 8 = 18 = PB
  3. Subtract PA: 18 - 8 = 10 = AB

Use power of point P: PT² = PA·PB

  1. Power: PT² = PA × PB
  2. 144 = 8 × PB → PB = 18
  3. Chord AB = PB - PA = 10

RBIQuantitative Aptitudeverified2026-11-22
What is the unit digit of the expression \((347)^{89} \times (562)^{103} \times (789)^{56}\)?
A. 2B. 4C. 6D. 8
Answer: C
Cyclicity Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use known unit digit cycles for 7, 2, 9

  1. Find unit digit of each base: 7, 2, 9.
  2. Reduce exponents modulo cycle length: 89 mod 4 = 1, 103 mod 4 = 3, 56 mod 2 = 0.
  3. Compute unit digits: 7^1 = 7, 2^3 = 8, 9^0 = 1.
  4. Multiply unit digits: 7 × 8 = 56 → 6, 6 × 1 = 6.
More methods (1)
Modulo 10 Product~6sshortcut

Compute each term modulo 10 using exponent reduction

  1. 347^89 mod 10 = 7^89 mod 10. Since 7^4 ≡ 1, 7^89 = 7^(4×22+1) ≡ 7.
  2. 562^103 mod 10 = 2^103 mod 10. Since 2^4 ≡ 6? Actually cycle 4: 2^103 = 2^(4×25+3) ≡ 2^3 = 8.
  3. 789^56 mod 10 = 9^56 mod 10. Since 9^2 ≡ 1, 9^56 ≡ 1.
  4. Multiply residues: 7 × 8 × 1 = 56 ≡ 6 mod 10.

CGLQuantitative Aptitudeverified2026-11-22
A solid metallic cylinder of radius 6 cm and height 10 cm is melted and recast into solid spheres of radius 3 cm each. The number of spheres formed is:
A. 8B. 9C. 10D. 12
Answer: C
Dimension Ratio Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use radius ratio to cancel π and simplify

  1. Note cylinder radius = 2 × sphere radius (6 = 2×3).
  2. Cylinder volume = π(2r)²h = 4πr²h.
  3. Sphere volume = (4/3)πr³.
  4. Divide: (4πr²h) / ((4/3)πr³) = 3h/r.
  5. Plug h=10, r=3 → 30/3 = 10 spheres.
More methods (2)
Quick Mental Math~8smnemonic

Cancel π, compute numbers mentally

  1. Cancel π from both volumes.
  2. Cylinder: 6×6×10 = 360.
  3. Sphere: (4/3)×27 = 36.
  4. 360 ÷ 36 = 10.
Direct Volume Division~10sshortcut

Compute volumes exactly and divide

  1. Cylinder volume = π × 6² × 10 = 360π.
  2. Sphere volume = (4/3)π × 3³ = 36π.
  3. Number = 360π / 36π = 10.

CGLQuantitative Aptitudeverified2026-11-23
A boat takes 90 minutes less to travel 36 km downstream than to travel the same distance upstream. If the speed of the boat in still water is 10 km/h, what is the speed of the stream?
A. 2 km/hB. 3 km/hC. 4 km/hD. 5 km/h
Answer: A
Plug Options~10sshortcut

Test each choice directly

  1. For each option x, compute downstream time = 36/(10+x), upstream = 36/(10-x).
  2. Find difference upstream - downstream.
  3. The option giving 1.5 hours (90 min) is correct.
  4. Option A (2 km/h) gives 36/12=3h downstream, 36/8=4.5h upstream, diff=1.5h.
More methods (2)
Inverse Speed Trick~12sshortcut

Use reciprocal speeds to avoid fractions

  1. Time diff = 1.5h = 3/2 h. Distance = 36 km.
  2. 1/(10-x) - 1/(10+x) = (3/2)/36 = 1/24.
  3. Combine left: (2x)/(100-x^2) = 1/24 → 48x = 100 - x^2.
  4. Rearrange: x^2 + 48x - 100 = 0 → (x+50)(x-2)=0 → x=2.
Quick Quadratic~15sshortcut

Form and factor quadratic

  1. Let stream speed = x. Equation: 36/(10-x) - 36/(10+x) = 1.5.
  2. Simplify: 72x/(100-x^2) = 1.5 → 72x = 1.5(100-x^2).
  3. Divide 1.5: 48x = 100 - x^2 → x^2 + 48x - 100 = 0.
  4. Factor: (x+50)(x-2)=0 → x=2 km/h.

IBPS-POQuantitative Aptitudeverified2026-11-23
A boat takes 8 hours to travel a certain distance upstream and 6 hours to travel the same distance downstream. If the speed of the stream is 2 km/h, what is the speed of the boat in still water?
A. 12 km/hB. 14 km/hC. 16 km/hD. 18 km/h
Answer: B
Formula Shortcut~5sshortcut

Direct formula from time ratio

  1. Let t₁=8 upstream, t₂=6 downstream, s=2 km/h.
  2. Use b = s(t₁+t₂)/(t₁−t₂).
  3. Compute b = 2×(8+6)/(8−6) = 2×14/2 = 14 km/h.
More methods (2)
Speed Ratio Trick~6sshortcut

Invert time ratio to get speed ratio

  1. Time ratio upstream:downstream = 8:6 = 4:3.
  2. Speed ratio downstream:upstream = 4:3 (inverse).
  3. So (b+2)/(b−2) = 4/3.
  4. Cross-multiply: 3(b+2)=4(b−2) → 3b+6=4b−8 → b=14.
Quick Equation~8sshortcut

Set distances equal and solve

  1. Distance upstream = (b−2)×8.
  2. Distance downstream = (b+2)×6.
  3. Equate: 8(b−2)=6(b+2).
  4. 8b−16=6b+12 → 2b=28 → b=14.

  3. Multiply units: 3×9=27 → unit 7

  5. Add units: 7+8=15 → unit 5

Quantitative Techniques 3

CLATQuantitative Techniquesverified2026-11-10
A train travels at an average speed of 60 km/hr without stoppages and 40 km/hr with stoppages. How many minutes does the train stop per hour?
A. A. 15 minutesB. B. 20 minutesC. C. 25 minutesD. D. 30 minutes
Answer: B
Relative Speed Ratio~3sshortcut

Compare speeds to find time lost

  1. Find distance covered in 1 hour without stops: 60 km.
  2. Calculate actual distance covered with stops in same hour: 40 km.
  3. Difference is 20 km, which equals distance traveled while stopped.
  4. Time to cover 20 km at 60 km/hr is (20/60) hours = 1/3 hour.
  5. Convert 1/3 hour to minutes: 20 minutes.
  6. Final answer matches option B.
More methods (2)
Time Loss Formula~4sshortcut

Use simple time loss equation

  1. Recall formula: Stop Time = (V_no_stop - V_with_stop) / V_no_stop * 60 min.
  2. Substitute values: (60 - 40) / 60 * 60.
  3. Simplify: 20 / 60 * 60 = 20 minutes.
  4. Final answer matches option B.
Direct Proportion~5sestimate

Proportional reasoning on time lost

  1. In 1 hour without stops, train goes 60 km.
  2. With stops, it goes only 40 km in that same hour.
  3. Missing 20 km represents time spent stopped.
  4. At 60 km/hr, covering 20 km takes (20/60)*60 = 20 minutes.
  5. Final answer matches option B.

Difference equals 1% of principal at 10% for 2 years

  1. For 10% rate, CI-SI over 2 years = 1% of principal
  2. 1% of P = 50 => P = 50 * 100 = 5000

Scale from a base principal of 100

  1. For P=100, SI=20, CI=21, diff=1
  2. Diff 50 means principal = 100 * 50 = 5000

Simple Interest & Percentage 1

INVENTEDSimple Interest & Percentageverified2026-11-22
A bank offers a special 'Festival Savings Scheme' where the interest rate for the first year is 5% per annum on the principal. However, the interest earned in the first year is added to the principal at the end of the year. If a customer deposits ₹10,000 and closes the account after exactly one year, what is the total amount received?
A) ₹10,500B) ₹10,525C) ₹10,600D) ₹10,400
Answer: A) ₹10,500
Fractional Interest Method~10salternative
  1. Express 5% as fraction 1/20.
  2. Calculate interest: ₹10,000 × 1/20 = ₹500.
  3. Add interest to principal: ₹10,000 + ₹500 = ₹10,500.

Simple Interest and Investment Splitting 1

INVENTEDSimple Interest and Investment Splittingverified2026-11-23
A prudent investor splits a total sum of Rs. 20,000 into two parts. Part A is invested at 6% simple interest per annum, and Part B is invested at 8% simple interest per annum. If the combined annual interest earned from both parts is exactly equal to the simple interest on the entire Rs. 20,000 invested at a blended rate of 7% per annum for one year, what is the amount invested at 6%?
Rs. 10,000Rs. 15,000Rs. 8,000Rs. 12,000
Answer: Rs. 10,000
Weighted Average Method~10salternative
  1. Observe that the blended rate 7% is exactly the average of 6% and 8%.
  2. In a weighted average, if the average equals the midpoint, the weights (amounts) must be equal.
  3. Therefore each part is half of Rs. 20,000, i.e., Rs. 10,000.

If the train maintains the exact same average speed between these platforms as it did on the outbound journey, how much total time is required for the entire return trip?
2 hours2 hours 10 minutes2 hours 20 minutes2 hours 30 minutes
Answer: 2 hours 20 minutes
Delay-Distance Equivalence~10salternative
  1. Scheduled speed = 120 km / 120 min = 1 km/min
  2. Each 4-min delay equals 4 km extra distance; 5 delays add 20 km
  3. Effective distance = 140 km; time = 140 min = 2 hours 20 minutes

  1. Find the smallest multiple of 7 greater than 473: 7 × 68 = 476.
  2. Since 476 is a multiple of 7, 476 days ago was also a Monday.
  3. 473 days ago is 3 days after that Monday (476 - 473 = 3), which is Thursday.

Coding-Decoding with Date-Position Shift 1

INVENTEDCoding-Decoding with Date-Position Shiftverified2026-11-23
In a specific code language used for railway safety alerts, dates are encoded such that the letter corresponds to the sum of the Day's position in the month and its alphabetical value (A=1, B=2...), then converted back to a single letter based on the remainder when divided by 26. If the sum is > 26, subtract 26 once. Note: Only dates where the resulting letter is 'M' or later are considered valid alerts. On 10th March, an alert code is generated as 'L'. What date generates the alert code 'Q' under the same rules?
A) 13th AprilB) 20th MayC) 17th JuneD) 15th July
Answer: A) 13th April
Reverse Alphabetic Sum~12salternative
  1. Let the target letter be Q (17th in alphabet). Set up equation: day + monthAlphabetValue ≡ 17 (mod 26) with result ≥ 13 (M).
  2. For each month, use its alphabetical position (January=1, February=2, …). Compute required day = 17 – monthValue; if negative add 26.
  3. Check which computed day falls within that month’s possible days and also yields a letter ≥ M. Only April (value 4) gives day = 13, which is valid.
  4. Thus the date is 13th April.

Direction Sense (Path Folding) 1

INVENTEDDirection Sense (Path Folding)verified2026-11-22
A spy, Agent X, starts at point O facing North. He moves 20m forward, turns 90° Left, walks 15m, then turns 90° Left again and walks 20m. Finally, he turns 90° Left one more time and walks 15m to reach a hidden vault. If the Sun is at his back as he enters the vault, in which cardinal direction did Agent X originally face while walking along the LAST segment of his path?
A) NorthB) SouthC) WestD) East
Answer: D) East
Net Rotation Method~10salternative
  1. Three 90° left turns total 270° left, equivalent to a single 90° right turn
  2. Starting from North, a 90° right turn points East
  3. Thus the last segment is walked facing East

Direction Sense with Clockwise Turn Logic 1

INVENTEDDirection Sense with Clockwise Turn Logicverified2026-11-22
Point A is at 12 o'clock on a clock face. If you start walking from the center towards A, then make a sharp 'Right' turn (90 degrees), which direction are you now facing relative to the clock's frame?
3 o'clock position6 o'clock position9 o'clock position12 o'clock position
Answer: 3 o'clock position
Coordinate Rotation Method~10salternative
  1. Place the clock center at the origin with 12 o'clock on the positive y-axis.
  2. The initial walking direction is the vector (0,1).
  3. Rotate this vector 90° clockwise to obtain (1,0).
  4. The vector (1,0) points to the 3 o'clock position.

Direction Sense with Recursive Path Correction 1

INVENTEDDirection Sense with Recursive Path Correctionverified2026-11-21
A spy starts at a secure point 'X' facing North. His mission requires him to execute the following sequence of commands relative to his CURRENT direction, where every command rotates his internal compass by 90 degrees clockwise AFTER execution: 1. Move forward 5 units. 2. Turn Left (relative to current face) and move forward 5 units. 3. Turn Right (relative to current face) and move forward 5 units. 4. Turn Left (relative to current face) and move forward 5 units. However, due to a magnetic anomaly affecting Sector X, every time the spy executes a command that involves turning LEFT, his body rotates an additional 90 degrees clockwise immediately after the turn but BEFORE moving. If he turns RIGHT normally, no such rotation occurs. After completing all 4 steps, in which cardinal direction is the spy facing? A) North B) South C) East D) West
NorthSouthEastWest
Answer: East
Net Rotation Calculation~10salternative
  1. Determine net rotation per command: Move=+90°, Left=+90°, Right=+180°
  2. Sum rotations: 90+90+180+90=450°
  3. Modulo 360°: 450°≡90° (East)
  4. Final direction from North is East

Spot exact phrase reversal in options

  1. Read Premise 2: 'Some blue are green'.
  2. Look at Option II: 'Some green are blue'.
  3. Realize this is the same statement swapped subject/object.
  4. In syllogisms, swapping terms in a particular affirmative ('some') keeps it true.
  5. Option I introduces new Red-Green link not in text; reject it.
  6. Accept only II and choose option B.

Transitivity Check~3sinvariant

Verify direct link vs indirect gap

  1. Note: 'Some A are B' creates no fixed relation between A and C.

  3. Conclusion I claims Red-Green link; none exists without extra info.
  4. Conclusion II repeats the premise 'Some blue are green' reversed.
  5. Since Green-Blue link is explicitly stated, Conclusion II follows.
  6. Conclude only II is true and select option B.

Draw minimal circles for premises

  3. Place 'Some blue are green' by overlapping Blue and Green.

  6. Select option B because only Conclusion II is valid.

CGLGeneral Intelligence & Reasoningneeds review2026-11-12
Statements (Coded): X # Y, Y @ Z, Z % W (#=Some, @=No, %=All). Conclusions: I. Some X are not Z. II. No W is Y.

The Scientist and Verifier disagreed; this method is kept for review, not published as verified.

Subset Logic Chain~3sshortcut

Trace containment rules

  1. Translate 'Y @ Z' to Y disjoint from Z.
  2. Translate 'Z % W' to Z subset of W.
  3. Deduce Y disjoint from W, making 'No W is Y' true.
  4. Deduce part of X must be in Z (from I's negation context), making I false.
  5. Select option B.
More methods (2)
Direct Venn Mapping~4sinvariant

Draw quick circles for sets

  1. Map 'X # Y' as overlapping X and Y circles.
  2. Map 'Y @ Z' (No) as separate Y and Z circles.
  3. Map 'Z % W' (All) as Z inside W circle.
  4. Check I: Overlap exists, so 'Some X not Z' is false (part of X is in Z).
  5. Check II: Y and Z are separate; since Z is in W, all Y is outside W. True.
Symbol Elimination~5sestimate

Remove irrelevant links

  1. Ignore # relation for checking II directly.
  2. Focus on @ and % to find W vs Y status.
  3. See @ makes Y separate from Z; % puts Z in W.
  4. Conclude Y is fully outside W, validating conclusion II.
  5. Validate I fails because X overlaps Z, so 'Some not Z' isn't necessarily true.
  6. Choose B.

Answer: D

identify missing logical bridge

  1. Identify the gap between 'Cats' and 'Pets'.
  2. Check if a direct link exists in premises.
  3. Find no statement saying 'All mammals are pets' or linking Cats directly.
  4. Acknowledge the logical gap prevents definite inference.
  5. Select option stating indeterminacy.
More methods (2)
Syllogism Rule Recall~3smnemonic

remember the 'some-some' gap rule

  1. Recall: Two 'some' premises yield no conclusion.
  2. Here: Premise 2 is 'some mammals are pets'.
  3. Premise 1 links cats to all mammals.
  4. Since 'pets' only touch 'mammals', they might miss 'cats'.
  5. Result: Cannot determine if cats are pets.

visualize set overlaps quickly

  2. Place 'All Cats' entirely inside 'Mammals'.

AGNIVEERReasoningverified2026-11-15
If all 'Clouds' are 'Rain', and some 'Rain' is 'Wind', then which of the following conclusions logically follows? I. Some Clouds are Wind. II. Some Rain is not Cloud.
A. Only I followsB. Only II followsC. Either I or II followsD. Neither I nor II follows
Answer: D
Rule Check~4smnemonic

Apply syllogism distribution rules

  1. Premises: All C are R (A), Some R are W (I).
  2. Middle term R undistributed in both premises.
  3. Undistributed middle → no valid conclusion.
  4. Conclusion I (Some C are W) would need distributed middle.
  5. Conclusion II (Some R not C) is O; not derivable from A.
  6. Thus neither follows.
More methods (2)
Venn Snap~5sshortcut

Mental Venn diagram shows no forced overlap

  1. Draw Clouds circle inside Rain circle.
  2. Draw Wind circle overlapping Rain but not necessarily Clouds.
  3. Observe Cloud-Wind overlap not guaranteed.
  4. Observe Rain-not-Cloud area not guaranteed (Clouds could fill Rain).
  5. Conclude neither conclusion must be true.
Counterexample Test~6sinvariant

Build models where premises hold but each conclusion fails

  1. For I false: Clouds={c}, Rain={c,r}, Wind={r}. All C are R, Some R are W, but no C is W.
  2. For II false: Clouds={c}, Rain={c}, Wind={c}. All C are R, Some R are W, but all Rain are Clouds.
  3. Since each conclusion can be false while premises true, neither logically follows.

Seven persons P, Q, R, S, T, U, and V live on seven different floors of a building, numbered 1 to 7 (1 being the ground floor). Only one person lives on each floor. U lives on floor 4. P lives on an even-numbered floor. There are two floors between P and Q, and Q lives below P. R lives on the floor immediately above S. T lives on an odd-numbered floor above R. V does not live on the topmost floor. Who lives on the 5th floor?
A. PB. QC. VD. T
Answer: C
Even-Odd Filter~8smnemonic

Use parity and ordering to quickly eliminate impossible floors

  1. U=4 fixed.
  2. P even (2,6); gap of 2 floors with Q below P ⇒ P=6, Q=3.
  3. Free floors: 1,2,5,7. R=S+1 ⇒ only (1,2) available ⇒ S=1, R=2.
  4. T odd > R ⇒ T∈{5,7}.
  5. V≠7 ⇒ V=5.
More methods (1)
Anchor & Propagate~10sshortcut

Fix knowns, then cascade constraints to isolate floor 5

  1. Place U on floor 4.
  2. P even → 2 or 6; two floors between P and Q with Q below P forces P=6, Q=3.
  3. Remaining floors: 1,2,5,7. R immediately above S → only consecutive pair (1,2) works → S=1, R=2.
  4. T on odd floor above R (2) → T=5 or 7.
  5. V not on top (7) → V=5, T=7.

Conclusion II: Some doctors are not artists. Which of the following is correct? A. Both I and II follow. B. Only I follows. C. Only II follows. D. Neither I nor II follows.
A. Both I and II followB. Only I followsC. Only II followsD. Neither I nor II follows
Answer: A · paper key: D

The lab answer differs from the answer key in the paper; kept for review.

Rule Trigger~4sinvariant

Apply standard syllogism rules: universal + particular gives particular; negative universal + particular gives negative particular

  1. For I: All architects are engineers (All A are E) + Some doctors are architects (Some D are A) → architects exist → Some engineers are architects (Some E are A)
  2. For II: No artist is doctor (No Art are D) + Some doctors are architects (Some D are A) → doctors exist → Some doctors are not artists (Some D are not Art)
  3. Both valid → Answer A
More methods (1)
Subset Existence~5sshortcut

Use existential import from 'some' to activate universal and disjointness

  1. Statement 1: All architects are engineers → architects ⊆ engineers
  2. Statement 4: Some doctors are architects → architects exist
  3. Therefore some engineers are architects (I follows)
  4. Statement 3: No artist is a doctor → artists and doctors disjoint
  5. Statement 4: Some doctors exist → those doctors are not artists (II follows)
  6. Both follow → Answer A

RBIReasoningverified2026-11-22
In a certain coded language: 'P + Q' means 'P is the father of Q'; 'P - Q' means 'P is the mother of Q'; 'P * Q' means 'P is the brother of Q'; 'P / Q' means 'P is the sister of Q'; 'P % Q' means 'P is the husband of Q'; 'P $ Q' means 'P is the wife of Q'. Based on the above codes, in the expression **'P + Q - R * S % T'**, how is **P** related to **T**?
A. GrandfatherB. Grandfather-in-lawC. Father-in-lawD. Uncle
Answer: B
Generation & Marriage Count~5sestimate

Count blood generations from P to spouse of T, then add in-law.

  1. P to Q: 1 gen (father)
  2. Q to R/S: 1 gen (mother) → P to S = 2 gens (grandfather)
  3. S % T: S is husband of T → T is wife of S
  4. Grandfather of spouse = grandfather-in-law
  5. Thus P is grandfather-in-law of T
More methods (2)
Gender & Role Tracking~6smnemonic

Track genders and marital links to spot in-law connection fast.

  1. + = father (male), - = mother (female)
  2. * = brother (male), % = husband (male)
  3. $ = wife (female), / = sister (female)
  4. P male, Q female, R male, S male, T female
  5. Blood: P→Q→R/S (2 gens), Marriage: S–T
  6. P is grandfather of S → grandfather-in-law of T
Chain Decoding~8sshortcut

Decode each symbol left-to-right building a mini family tree.

  1. P + Q → P father of Q
  2. Q - R → Q mother of R (Q female)
  3. R * S → R brother of S (R male, S sibling)
  4. S % T → S husband of T (S male, T wife)
  5. Q mother of S → P grandfather of S
  6. P grandfather of S → P grandfather-in-law of T
