In a row of students, A is 7th from the left and B is 9th from the right. If they interchange positions, A becomes 11th from the left. How many students are there in the row?
A. 18B. 19C. 20D. 21
Answer: B
Swap Sum Shortcut~4sshortcut

Add A's new left rank and B's original right rank, subtract 1

  1. After interchange, A is 11th from left → this was B's original left rank.
  2. B was originally 9th from right.
  3. Total students = left rank + right rank - 1 = 11 + 9 - 1 = 19.
More methods (2)
Left-Right Invariant~5sinvariant

For any student, left position + right position - 1 = total

  1. After swap, A occupies B's original spot, so A's new left (11) = B's original left.
  2. B's original right = 9.
  3. Apply invariant: total = 11 + 9 - 1 = 19.
Option Check~6sestimate

Plug each option into B's left rank formula; only B fits

  1. For a total N, B's left rank = N - 9 + 1 = N - 8.
  2. After swap, A's left rank becomes B's left rank = N - 8.
  3. Given A's new left rank = 11, so N - 8 = 11 → N = 19.

Sum orthogonal components separately

  1. Assign axes: North=+y, East=+x

In an election, 75% of eligible voters participated. Of these, 4% were inv alid votes. If a winning candidat egot 13,500 votes which was 55% of valid votes, what was the total number of eligible voterstoo the near est int eger)?
A. 30,000B. 35,050C. 34091D. 45,024
Answer: C

The Scientist and Verifier disagreed; this method is kept for review, not published as verified.

Reverse Fraction Chain~5sshortcut

Chain valid vote fractions to find total eligible.

  1. Calculate valid votes: 13,500 is 55% of valid, so valid = 13500 * 100/55 = 24,545.
  2. Calculate turnout fraction: 75% participated, 4% invalid means 96% of turnout were valid.
  3. Total Eligible = Valid / (Turnout * 0.96) = 24545 / 0.72 ≈ 34091.
More methods (1)
Direct Algebra~6sinvariant

Set up one equation linking eligible voters to winner's votes.

  1. Let E be eligible voters. Valid votes = 0.75 * E * 0.96.
  2. Winner's votes = 0.55 * (Valid votes) = 13,500.
  3. Solve: E = 13500 / (0.55 * 0.75 * 0.96) ≈ 34091.

Difference Equation~5sshortcut

Syllogism 1

INVENTEDSyllogismverified2026-11-22
In a certain community, all 'Kartars' are also 'Yogis'. No 'Yogi' is a 'Vedantist'. Some 'Vedantists' are 'Brahmins'. What must be true? I. All Kartars are Brahmins. II. No Kartar is a Vedantist.
Only I followsOnly II followsBoth I and II followNeither I nor II follows
Answer: Only II follows
Contradiction and Counterexample~10salternative
  1. Assume a Kartar is a Vedantist; then that Kartar is a Yogi (all Kartars are Yogis), contradicting 'No Yogi is a Vedantist'. Hence II must be true.
  2. Construct a model: one Kartar (also Yogi), one Vedantist (also Brahmin), with Yogis and Vedantists disjoint. All premises hold but Kartar is not a Brahmin, so I does not follow.
  3. Therefore only statement II follows.

In this specific context, what does the idiom 'the silent witness' most logically refer to? A. The anonymity of passengers who refuse to speak. B. The anonymity of passengers who refuse to speak.The natural environment (wind, weather) that historically observed the station without human intervention.A specific legal doctrine regarding non-human evidence in criminal law.The internal monologue of the station master during a panic situation.

The most abundant element in Earth's crust is:
A. IronB. OxygenC. SiliconD. Aluminum
Answer: B
Crust Composition Rule~2smnemonic

Recall top 3 elements directly

  1. Identify Earth's crust as the target layer.
  2. Recall Oxygen is ~46% and Silicon is ~28% of mass.
  3. Eliminate Iron, Aluminum as less abundant than Oxygen.
  4. Select Option B for Oxygen.
More methods (2)
Abundance Ranking Shortcut~3sshortcut

Use known ranking order

  1. Access memory of Earth crust element abundance order.
  2. Remember sequence: Oxygen > Silicon > Aluminum > Iron.
  3. Compare options against this fixed sequence.
  4. Confirm Oxygen (Option B) is first in list.
Mass Fraction Elimination~4sestimate

Filter by percentage knowledge

  1. Recall Oxygen comprises nearly half crust mass.
  2. Note Silicon and Aluminum are significantly less than 50%.
  3. Discard Iron as minor component compared to top two.
  4. Choose Option B representing the dominant element.

JEEChemistryverified2026-11-13
The pH of 0.001 M NaOH is approximately:
A. 3B. 7C. 10D. 11
Answer: D
pH Scale Estimate~2sestimate

Count zeros in molarity

  1. Note concentration is 10^-3 M.
  2. Recall strong bases give pOH equal to negative exponent (3).
  3. Estimate pH as 14 minus that pOH value.
  4. Determine result is 11.
  5. Choose option D.
More methods (2)
Strong Base Logic~3sinvariant

NaOH fully dissociates

  1. Identify NaOH as a strong base.
  2. Recognize 0.001 M concentration yields [OH-] = 0.001 M.
  3. Calculate pOH = -log(0.001) = 3.
  4. Subtract from 14 to get pH = 11.
  5. Select option D.
Inverse Acid Rule~4sshortcut

pH + pOH = 14

  1. Start with known pH+pOH=14 constant.
  2. Convert 0.001 M to pOH of 3 instantly.
  3. Apply formula: 14 - 3 = 11.
  4. Verify answer matches option D.
  5. Confirm choice is D.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
JEEChemistryverified2026-11-13
The oxidation state of Mn in KMnO4 is:
A. +5B. +6C. +7D. +4
Answer: C
MnO4- Group~2smnemonic

Treat permanganate as unit

  1. Identify compound as K+ and MnO4- ion.
  2. Recall standard charge of permanganate is -1.
  3. Add cation charge (+1) to anion charge (-1) = 0.
  4. Isolate Mn: +1 (K) + x (Mn) + (-8) (O) = 0 -> x=+7.
More methods (2)
Algebraic Balance~3sinvariant

Sum of charges equals zero

  1. Assign K as +1 and O as -2.
  2. Calculate total oxygen charge: 4 * (-2) = -8.
  3. Set equation: (+1) + Mn + (-8) = 0.
  4. Solve for Mn: Mn = +7.
Quick Oxygen Rule~4sestimate

Subtract oxygen deficit from zero

  1. Count 4 Oxygen atoms at -2 each (total -8).
  2. Recognize Potassium is +1.
  3. Sum knowns: +1 + (-8) = -7.
  4. Mn must be +7 to neutralize the -7 net charge.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper
JEEChemistryverified2026-11-13
The coordination number in NaCl crystal structure is:
A. 4B. 6C. 8D. 12
Answer: B
Lattice Invariant~2sinvariant

Use standard crystal data

  1. Recognize NaCl structure from name or context.
  2. Recall standard coordination number for rock salt is fixed at 6.
  3. No calculation needed; this is a known constant.
  4. Select option corresponding to value 6.
More methods (2)
Octahedral Rule~3smnemonic

NaCl forms octahedral holes

  1. Identify NaCl as a rock salt structure.
  2. Recall ions occupy octahedral voids in FCC lattice.
  3. An octahedron has exactly 6 vertices.
  4. Each ion touches 6 oppositely charged neighbors.
Space-Filling Count~4sestimate

Visualize nearest neighbors

  1. Imagine a central Na+ ion surrounded by Cl- ions.
  2. Count the ions touching it directly along axes.
  3. There are 3 in the plane, plus 1 above and 1 below.
  4. Total count equals 6 nearest neighbors.

Noble gases have highest IE

Small radius equals high IE

  4. Conclude Helium has the highest ionization energy.
  5. Final answer is C. He.

  3. Rightmost column (noble gases) has highest values.
  4. He is at the very top of this group, making it highest overall.

Use the inverse log relationship for strong bases directly.

  3. Subtract pOH from 14 to get pH = 13.

  3. Calculate pOH = -log(0.1) which is 1, then pH = 14 - 1 = 13.
  4. Select option C.

  2. Deduce that 0.1 M (the geometric mean) must have pH 13.

What is the type of hybridization of the central atom in the molecule CO2?
A. spB. sp2C. sp3D. dsp2
Answer: A
Linear Geometry Check~2smnemonic

CO2 shape dictates hybridization instantly

  1. 1. Recall CO2 is a linear molecule (O=C=O).
  2. 2. Linear geometry requires exactly two electron domains.
  3. 3. Two domains correspond to sp hybridization.
  4. 4. Ignore lone pairs or d-orbitals for this case.
  5. 5. Choose option A.
More methods (2)
Count Sigma Bonds~3sinvariant

sigma bonds determine hybridization directly

  1. 1. Identify central atom: Carbon in CO2.
  2. 2. Count sigma bonds: C forms two double bonds (2 sigma).
  3. 3. Lone pairs on C: 0.
  4. 4. Hybridization = number of electron domains (2) → sp.
  5. 5. Select option A.
Oxygen Rule~4sshortcut

double bonded oxygens imply sp

  1. 1. Note all bonds to O are double bonds.
  2. 2. Each double bond removes one p-orbital from C.
  3. 3. Only two sigma orbitals remain for hybridization.
  4. 4. Two hybrids = sp configuration.
  5. 5. Match with option A.

6 g of a non-electrolyte solute dissolved in 100 g of water elevates the boiling point by 0.26 K. The molar mass of the solute is: (Kb for water = 0.52 K kg mol⁻¹)
A. 30 g/molB. 60 g/molC. 120 g/molD. 240 g/mol
Answer: C
Ratio Shortcut~5sshortcut

Use Kb/ΔTb ratio to cancel decimals

  1. Compute Kb/ΔTb = 0.52/0.26 = 2
  2. Multiply by mass/solvent kg = 6/0.1 = 60
  3. M = 2 × 60 = 120 g/mol
More methods (2)
Molality First~5sshortcut

Find molality directly from ΔTb/Kb

  1. Molality = ΔTb/Kb = 0.26/0.52 = 0.5 mol/kg
  2. Moles = 0.5 × 0.1 kg = 0.05 mol
  3. Molar mass = 6 g / 0.05 mol = 120 g/mol
Formula Plug~6sshortcut

Direct rearranged formula M = (Kb×mass)/(ΔTb×kg solvent)

  1. Numerator = 0.52 × 6 = 3.12
  2. Denominator = 0.26 × 0.1 = 0.026
  3. Divide: 3.12 / 0.026 = 120

A. ZeroB. FirstC. SecondD. Third

Order-unit pattern match~2smnemonic

Memorize common units: zero=mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹, first=s⁻¹, second=L mol⁻¹ s⁻¹, third=L² mol⁻² s⁻¹

  1. Recognize L² mol⁻² s⁻¹ matches third order pattern
  2. Select third order
More methods (2)
Unit exponent shortcut~3sshortcut

Convert unit to (L/mol)^power and add 1

  1. Write unit as L² mol⁻² s⁻¹ = (L/mol)² s⁻¹
  2. Exponent 2 equals n−1 for nth order
  3. Solve n−1=2 → n=3
Concentration power rule~4sshortcut

Use rate constant unit formula conc^(1−n) time⁻¹

  1. Rate constant unit = (mol/L)^(1−n) s⁻¹
  2. Given L² mol⁻² s⁻¹ = (L/mol)² s⁻¹ = (mol/L)^(−2) s⁻¹
  3. Set 1−n = −2 → n=3

NEETChemistryverified2026-11-17
The pH of a 1.0 × 10⁻⁸ M aqueous HCl solution at 298 K is approximately:
A. 8.00B. 7.00C. 6.98D. 8.02
Answer: C
Invariant lookup~3smnemonic

Recall that for C ≤ 10⁻⁶ M, pH ≈ 6.98

  1. Recognize C = 1×10⁻⁸ M is far below 10⁻⁶ M
  2. Remember the standard result: pH ≈ 6.98
More methods (2)
Kw correction~5sshortcut

Add water auto‑ionization to acid concentration

  1. Write charge balance: [H+] = (C + √(C²+4K_w))/2
  2. Insert C = 1.0×10⁻⁸ M, K_w = 1.0×10⁻¹⁴
  3. Compute √(C²+4K_w) ≈ 2.00×10⁻⁷
  4. Find [H+] ≈ (1.0×10⁻⁸ + 2.00×10⁻⁷)/2 = 1.05×10⁻⁷ M
  5. pH = –log₁₀[H+] ≈ 6.98
Log approx~5sestimate

Use small‑C expansion of log

  1. Pure water gives [H+] = 1.0×10⁻⁷ M → pH = 7.00
  2. Add acid: total [H+] ≈ 1.0×10⁻⁷ M (1 + C/1.0×10⁻⁷)
  3. C/1.0×10⁻⁷ = 0.1, so [H+] ≈ 1.1×10⁻⁷ M
  4. pH ≈ 7 – log₁₀1.1 ≈ 7 – 0.041 = 6.96 ≈ 6.98

  1. Standard EMF = 1.10 V
  2. Q = 10 (one decade)
  3. Voltage shift = 0.059/2 = 0.03 V per decade
  4. Subtract: 1.10 - 0.03 = 1.07 V
Nernst Shortcut~5sshortcut

Direct Nernst with mental log10(10)=1

  1. E° = 0.34 - (-0.76) = 1.10 V
  2. Q = [Zn²⁺]/[Cu²⁺] = 0.1/0.01 = 10
  3. ΔE = (0.059/2) × log10(10) = 0.0295 V
  4. E = 1.10 - 0.03 ≈ 1.07 V

  4. C: CH3COCH3 has CH3CO- → positive
  5. D: C6H5CHO has no CH3 on carbonyl carbon → negative

  4. B: Acetaldehyde → CH3CHO (alpha-CH3) ✓

Only 3 classes give positive: methyl ketones, acetaldehyde, ethanol-type

  5. Class 4: Everything else → benzaldehyde is aromatic aldehyde, not in list ✗

Use volume conservation

  1. Recall resistance R = ρL/A.

  3. New resistance R' scales with length ratio (2) and inverse area ratio (2), giving 4R.

Separate length and area effects

  1. Length L doubles, which multiplies resistance by factor 2.
  2. Constant volume means if L doubles, cross-section A must halve.
  3. Halving area doubles resistance again; combined effect is 2 * 2 = 4R.

Compare new to old directly

  1. Start with formula R ∝ L/A.
  2. Since V=LA is fixed, A ∝ 1/L, so R ∝ L².
  3. If L becomes 2L, then R becomes (2)² = 4 times original.

JEEPhysicsverified2026-11-13
The momentum of a photon of energy E is:
A. E/c²B. E/cC. EcD. hE/c
Answer: B
Energy-Momentum Relation~5sinvariant

Use photon invariant mass zero

  1. Recall photons have zero rest mass (m=0).
  2. Apply relativistic energy-momentum relation E²=(pc)²+(mc²)².
  3. Substitute m=0 to get E²=(pc)², so E=pc.
  4. Solve for momentum p by dividing both sides by c.
  5. Result: p=E/c, which matches option B.
More methods (2)
De Broglie Shortcut~5sshortcut

Combine Planck's relation with wave speed

  1. Write photon energy as E=hf where f is frequency.
  2. Use wavelength relation λ=c/f for light in vacuum.
  3. Recall de Broglie momentum formula p=h/λ.
  4. Substitute f=E/h and λ=c/f into p=h/λ to get p=h/(c/f).
  5. Simplify expression: p=hf/c, then replace hf with E.
  6. Result: p=E/c, which matches option B.
Dimensional Analysis~5sestimate

Check units of momentum vs energy

  1. Identify units: Momentum is kg·m/s, Energy is Joules (kg·m²/s²).
  2. Note speed of light c has units m/s.
  3. Test option A (E/c²): Units become J/(m²/s²) = kg, incorrect for momentum.
  4. Test option B (E/c): Units become J/(m/s) = kg·m/s, correct.
  5. Verify other options yield incorrect dimensional combinations.
  6. Result: Only E/c has correct units, matching option B.
Checked by an independent solve · original to this lab · source paper

A thermodynamic system undergoes a process where the internal energy change is 100 J and the heat added to the system is 200 J. What is the work done by the system?
A. -100 JB. 100 JC. 200 JD. 300 J
Answer: B · paper key: A

The lab answer differs from the answer key in the paper; kept for review.

Energy Balance Shortcut~4sestimate

Balance input vs output

  1. Total energy entering system is heat Q = 200 J.
  2. Net energy stored is internal change ΔU = 100 J.
  3. Remaining energy must leave as work done BY system.
  4. Calculate difference: 200 J - 100 J = 100 J.
  5. Match result to option B.
More methods (2)
First Law Direct~5sshortcut

Apply U=Q-W directly

  1. Identify internal energy change ΔU = 100 J.
  2. Identify heat added Q = 200 J.
  3. Rearrange formula W = Q - ΔU to 200 - 100.
  4. Calculate result W = 100 J.
  5. Select option B.
Sign Convention Check~6smnemonic

Ensure work is by system

  1. Recall standard sign convention: Q positive for heat in.
  2. Recall standard sign convention: ΔU positive for energy gain.
  3. Apply First Law: ΔU = Q - W (where W is work done BY system).
  4. Substitute values: 100 = 200 - W.
  5. Solve for W: W = 100 J.
  6. Confirm option B matches positive work output.

A uniform disc of mass M and radius R has a circular hole of radius R/2 cut out from it, touching the disc's circumference. The moment of inertia of the remaining part about an axis passing through the centre of the original disc and perpendicular to its plane is:
A. MR²/2B. 13MR²/32C. 3MR²/8D. MR²/4
Answer: B
Negative Mass~8sshortcut

Treat hole as negative mass; sum MOI of full disc and negative hole

  1. Full disc: mass M, MOI = ½MR²
  2. Hole: mass -M/4 at distance R/2 from O
  3. Hole MOI about O = -[½(M/4)(R/2)² + (M/4)(R/2)²] = -3MR²/32
  4. Total MOI = ½MR² - 3MR²/32 = 13MR²/32
More methods (1)
Subtract Hole~10sshortcut

Subtract hole's MOI from full disc using area ratio and parallel axis

  1. Full disc MOI = ½MR²
  2. Hole radius R/2 → area ¼ → mass M/4
  3. Hole MOI about its center = ½(M/4)(R/2)² = MR²/32
  4. Parallel axis: add (M/4)(R/2)² = MR²/16 → hole MOI about O = 3MR²/32
  5. Remaining MOI = ½MR² - 3MR²/32 = 13MR²/32

  3. Ratio = (2⁻³)^(2/3) = 2⁻² = 1/4

Volume-Temperature Scaling~4smnemonic

  3. Temperature ratio = (2³)^(-2/3) = 2⁻² = 1/4

If it takes five machines five minutes to make five widgets, how long would it take 100 machines to make 100 widgets?
A. 5 minutesB. 10 minutesC. 50 minutesD. 100 minutes
Answer: A
Parallel Scaling~2sshortcut

Machines and widgets scale equally, time stays constant

  1. Both machines and widgets ×20 (5→100)
  2. Work per machine unchanged
  3. Time unchanged at 5 minutes
More methods (2)
Invariant Rate~3sinvariant

Each machine makes 1 widget in 5 minutes regardless of count

  1. 5 machines → 5 widgets in 5 min
  2. So 1 machine → 1 widget in 5 min
  3. 100 machines → 100 widgets in 5 min
Unit Rate Check~5sestimate

Compute widgets per machine-minute to verify

  1. Rate = 5 widgets / (5×5) = 0.2 per machine-min
  2. Time = 100 / (100 × 0.2) = 5 min

If a car travels from city A to city B at an average speed of 60 km/h and returns at an average speed of 40 km/h, what is the average speed for the entire trip?
A. 48 km/hB. 50 km/hC. 55 km/hD. 60 km/h
Answer: A
Harmonic Mean~5sshortcut

Use 2ab/(a+b) for equal distances

  1. Multiply speeds: 60×40=2400
  2. Double it: 4800
  3. Sum speeds: 60+40=100
  4. Divide: 4800/100=48
More methods (2)
Ratio Shortcut~6smnemonic

Average speed = 2/(1/60+1/40)

  1. Reciprocals: 1/60, 1/40
  2. Sum: (2+3)/120=5/120=1/24
  3. Double reciprocal: 2/(1/24)=48
Assume Distance~7sinvariant

Pick LCM distance to avoid fractions

  1. Let distance = 120 km (LCM of 60,40)
  2. Time there: 120/60=2 h
  3. Time back: 120/40=3 h
  4. Total dist: 240 km, total time: 5 h
  5. Avg = 240/5=48

A company has 15 employees, and each employee can speak two languages. What is the minimum number of languages that the company must have to satisfy this condition?
A. 8 languagesB. 10 languagesC. 12 languagesD. 15 languages
Answer: A

The Scientist and Verifier disagreed; this method is kept for review, not published as verified.

Simple Count~3smnemonic

Direct counting of required distinct languages

  1. Total language slots = 30.
  2. Each language contributes at most 2 slots → minimum languages = 30 ÷ 2 = 15, but overlap reduces to ⌈15/2⌉ = 8.
  3. Answer is 8 languages.
More methods (2)
Half‑Plus‑One~4sshortcut

Half the employees plus one gives minimum distinct languages

  1. With 2 languages per person, each language can cover at most 2 employees.
  2. To cover 15 employees, need ⌈15/2⌉ = 8 distinct languages.
  3. No smaller number can satisfy the 30 slots requirement.
Pigeonhole Minimum~5sinvariant

Use pigeonhole principle to force overlap

  1. Each employee knows 2 languages → total language‑slots = 15 × 2 = 30.
  2. If there were 7 or fewer distinct languages, max slots without reuse = 7 × 2 = 14 < 30, impossible.
  3. Thus at least 8 languages are needed.

The lab answer differs from the answer key in the paper; kept for review.

Decimal Estimate~4sestimate

Approximate fractions to decimals for quick multiplication

  1. 5/10 = 0.5 exactly
  2. 3/9 ≈ 0.333
  3. 0.5 × 0.333 = 0.1665
  4. Closest option is 0.17 → B
More methods (2)
Fraction Multiply~5sshortcut

Multiply conditional probabilities directly

  1. Total balls = 10
  2. P(first red) = 5/10 = 1/2
  3. After red drawn, 9 balls left, 3 blue → P(second blue) = 3/9 = 1/3
  4. Multiply: (1/2)*(1/3) = 1/6 ≈ 0.1667
  5. Match to option B (0.17)
Ordered Count~5sshortcut

Count favorable ordered draws over total ordered draws

  1. Favorable ordered draws: 5 red × 3 blue = 15
  2. Total ordered draws: 10 × 9 = 90
  3. Probability = 15/90 = 1/6 ≈ 0.1667
  4. Select option B (0.17)

A snail is at the bottom of a 20-foot well. Each day, it climbs up 3 feet, but at night, it slips back 2 feet. How many days will it take for the snail to reach the top of the well?
A. 18 daysB. 20 daysC. 22 daysD. 25 days
Answer: A
Net Gain + Final Day~5sshortcut

Subtract last climb, divide by net gain, add final day

  1. Subtract last day's climb: 20 - 3 = 17 feet remaining
  2. Divide by net daily gain (3-2=1): 17 / 1 = 17 days
  3. Add the final day: 17 + 1 = 18 days
More methods (2)
Inequality Setup~5sshortcut

Model position before final climb

  1. Let n = total days. Before day n, snail climbed (n-1) net feet
  2. On day n it climbs 3 ft: (n-1) + 3 >= 20
  3. Solve: n-1 >= 17 => n >= 18
Milestone Check~5sestimate

Find day when snail reaches 17 ft before final climb

  1. After 17 days, snail at 17 ft (net 1 ft/day)
  2. On day 18, climbs 3 ft to 20 ft, exits well
  3. No slip back needed

For similar figures, perimeter ratio equals square root of area ratio

Answer: B
Direct Pair Difference~4smnemonic

use formula 2A = (A+B)+(A+C)-(B+C)

  1. Compute 2A = 1/6 + 1/12 - 1/8
  2. 1/6+1/12 = 1/4, minus 1/8 = 1/8
  3. So 2A = 1/8 => A = 1/16 per day
  4. A alone takes 16 days
More methods (2)
LCM Units~5sshortcut

convert to units per day using LCM

  1. Take LCM of 6,8,12 = 24 as total work
  2. A+B=4, B+C=3, C+A=2 units/day
  3. Add: 2(A+B+C)=9 => A+B+C=4.5
  4. A = 4.5 - 3 = 1.5 units/day
  5. Time = 24/1.5 = 16 days
Rate Sum & Subtract~5sshortcut

work with fractions directly

  1. Add all three pair rates: 1/6+1/8+1/12 = 3/8
  2. Half gives combined rate: (A+B+C)=3/16 per day
  3. Subtract B+C rate: 3/16 - 1/8 = 1/16
  4. A's rate = 1/16 => 16 days

UPSCCSATverified2026-11-24
A train 150 m long crosses a platform 250 m long in 20 seconds. What is the speed of the train in km/h?
A. 54 km/hB. 72 km/hC. 60 km/hD. 90 km/h
Answer: B
Instant m/s to km/h~3smnemonic

Recognize 20 m/s equals 72 km/h directly

  1. Total distance 400 m in 20 s gives 20 m/s
  2. Recall 1 m/s = 3.6 km/h, so 20 m/s = 72 km/h
More methods (2)
Total Distance Shortcut~5sshortcut

Combine lengths, divide by time, convert with 3.6

  1. Add train and platform lengths: 150+250=400 m
  2. Divide by time: 400/20=20 m/s
  3. Multiply by 3.6: 20×3.6=72 km/h
Fraction Cancel~6sinvariant

Convert units in one fraction using 3600/1000

  1. Write speed = (150+250) m / 20 s
  2. Multiply by 3600/1000 = 3.6
  3. Compute: 400/20 × 3.6 = 20 × 3.6 = 72

UPSCCSATverified2026-11-24
What is the value of tan(45°)?
A. 0B. 1C. UndefinedD. None of these
Answer: B
Unit Circle Recall~5smnemonic

Instant recall of standard angle coordinates

  1. Visualize 45° on unit circle: coordinates (√2/2, √2/2)
  2. Compute tan = y/x = (√2/2)/(√2/2)
  3. Result is 1
More methods (2)
45-45-90 Triangle~5sshortcut

Use isosceles right triangle side ratios

  1. Sketch 45-45-90 triangle with legs = 1
  2. Hypotenuse = √2 by Pythagoras
  3. tan 45 = opposite/adjacent = 1/1 = 1
Sine/Cosine Ratio~5sinvariant

tan = sin/cos, both equal at 45°

  1. Recall sin 45° = √2/2
  2. Recall cos 45° = √2/2
  3. Divide: (√2/2) ÷ (√2/2) = 1

Remove non-safety goals quickly

  1. Note regulation targets 'risk' before deployment.
  2. Safety and accountability are standard risk responses.
  3. Cost, speed, and aesthetics do not address risk.
  4. Choose A as the correct completion.

Link 'risk assessment' to safety

  1. Identify 'risk assessment' implies risk mitigation.
  2. Map mitigation directly to safety and accountability.
  3. Eliminate unrelated options like cost or speed.
  4. Select option A as the only logical fit.
Principle Inference~4sinvariant

Deduce purpose from regulation name

  1. Recognize 'risk' inherently concerns harm prevention.
  2. Preventing harm requires safety measures and accountability.
  3. Reject options B, C, D as irrelevant to risk.
  4. Conclude A is the required principle.

CLATLegal Reasoningverified2026-11-10
A contract between Party A and Party B stipulates that Party A must deliver goods by Friday. However, an unforeseen flood destroys the factory where the goods were to be manufactured on Thursday. Under the doctrine of frustration of contract, what is the legal consequence regarding Party A's liability for non-delivery?
A. Party A is liable for breach of contract because the duty was absolute.B. Party A is not liable because the impossibility of performance was caused by an act of God (force majeure).C. Party A must pay damages to Party B but can claim reimbursement from the insurance company.D. Party A is automatically discharged from liability only if they had taken reasonable steps to mitigate the loss before the flood occurred.
Answer: B
Act of God Check~3sinvariant

Flood excuses liability

  1. Identify flood as an unforeseen event destroying the factory.
  2. Classify this as an 'act of God' preventing performance.
  3. Apply frustration doctrine: impossibility due to force majeure discharges liability.
More methods (2)
Frustration Shortcut~4sshortcut

Event makes duty impossible

  1. Check if the specific performance (making goods) became objectively impossible.
  2. Confirm the cause was a natural disaster (flood), not party fault.
  3. Conclude Party A is not liable for non-delivery under frustration.
Liability Filter~5sestimate

Absolute duty vs. excusing event

  1. Filter out 'absolute duty' (Option A) because force majeure exists.
  2. Filter out insurance claims (Option C) as the question asks about liability to B, not internal reimbursement.
  3. Select Option B: impossibility by act of God removes liability.

CLATLegal Reasoningverified2026-11-15
In a criminal trial, the prosecution presents a witness who testifies that they saw the accused commit the crime. However, it is later revealed that the witness had a motive to lie against the accused due to a personal grudge. How does this affect the admissibility of the testimony?
A. The testimony becomes inadmissible as hearsay because it relies on a biased source.B. The testimony remains admissible but carries less weight; the judge or jury may consider the bias during evaluation.C. The court must automatically dismiss the entire prosecution case due to the witness's credibility issue.D. The testimony is inadmissible unless corroborated by independent physical evidence.
Answer: B
Weight vs Admissibility~3sinvariant

Core evidence rule: bias affects credibility (weight), not admissibility

  1. Spot 'bias/grudge' → credibility issue
  2. Recall: credibility = weight for jury
  3. Eliminate A (hearsay error), C (auto-dismiss), D (corroboration rule)
  4. Select B: admissible, less weight
More methods (2)
Extreme Option Elimination~4sshortcut

Eliminate legally extreme/absolute answers; bias never auto-excludes or auto-dismisses

  1. Flag absolute words: 'inadmissible' (A,D), 'automatically dismiss' (C)
  2. Know bias goes to weight, not admissibility
  3. Only B reflects judicial discretion on weight
  4. Pick B
Hearsay vs Credibility~5smnemonic

Distinguish hearsay (out-of-court statement) from in-court witness bias

  1. Witness testifies in court → not hearsay
  2. A wrongly calls it hearsay
  3. Bias = credibility attack → jury weighs it
  4. B matches

Which of the following scenarios best illustrates the legal concept of 'Laches' as a defense against a claim for specific performance?

A plaintiff sues immediately after discovering a breach of contract.C. A defendant argues that the statute of limitations has expired based on the date of the breach.D. A plaintiff fails to produce documentary evidence despite having access to it for over a decade.

Laches is equitable defense; specific performance is equitable remedy

  3. Immediate suit (B) no delay.
  4. Missing evidence (D) not laches.

CLATLegal Reasoningverified2026-11-17
Parliament enacts the Constitution (Fiftieth Amendment) Act, inserting a new Article 329B which reads: 'Notwithstanding anything in this Constitution, no election to either House of Parliament or to the House or either House of the Legislature of a State shall be called in question in any Court of Law.' The Amendment is challenged in the Supreme Court on the ground that it violates the 'Basic Structure' of the Constitution.
A. The Amendment is invalid as it destroys the 'Basic Structure' of the Constitution by completely ousting judicial review in election matters, which is a fundamental feature of the Constitution.B. The Amendment is valid because Parliament's constituent power under Article 368 is plenary and unfettered, allowing it to amend any provision of the Constitution including those relating to elections.C. The Amendment is valid only if it has been ratified by the Legislatures of not less than one-half of the States as per the proviso to Article 368(2).D. The Amendment is invalid solely because it violates Article 14 (Right to Equality) by creating an arbitrary classification between election disputes and other disputes.
Answer: A
Basic Structure Test~5sshortcut

Immediately match amendment effect to core doctrine

  1. Amendment ousts all judicial review of elections.
  2. Judicial review is a basic structure feature (Kesavananda).
  3. Destroying basic structure makes amendment invalid.
More methods (2)
Constituent Power Limit~6smnemonic

Recall Parliament's amending power has inherent limits

  1. Art. 368 power is not plenary; basic structure is immune.
  2. Amendment inserts absolute bar on election challenges.
  3. This alters basic structure -> ultra vires.
Precedent Recall~7sinvariant

Link to landmark case striking down similar clause

  1. Identical to Art. 329A (39th Amendment) in Indira Gandhi v. Raj Narain.
  2. Supreme Court held ouster of judicial review violates basic structure.
  3. Same principle applies -> Amendment invalid.

CLATLegal Reasoningverified2026-11-17
Dr. Rao, a renowned cardiologist, visits City Hospital twice a week to perform surgeries. The hospital provides the operation theatre, nursing staff, anaesthetists, and equipment. During a surgery, Dr. Rao negligently leaves a surgical swab inside the patient, causing sepsis. The patient sues City Hospital for damages. The hospital contends that Dr. Rao is an independent contractor exercising his professional skill, for whose negligence the hospital cannot be held vicariously liable.
A. The hospital is not liable because Dr. Rao is an independent contractor exercising exclusive professional judgment, and the hospital lacked control over his surgical technique.B. The hospital is liable under the doctrine of vicarious liability as Dr. Rao was integrated into the hospital's functioning for patient care, and the hospital owes a non-delegable duty of care to its patients.C. The hospital is liable only if it is proven that the hospital administration was negligent in verifying Dr. Rao's medical credentials before granting him visiting privileges.D. The hospital is not liable because the negligence occurred during the exercise of Dr. Rao's specialized professional skill, which falls outside the hospital's right of control.
Answer: B
Non-delegable Duty Check~5sshortcut

Hospitals owe non-delegable duty to patients for care provided on premises.

  1. Spot 'hospital' and 'patient care' -> non-delegable duty arises.
  2. Independent contractor status irrelevant for non-delegable duties.
  3. Select option mentioning non-delegable duty.
More methods (2)
Integration Test~5sshortcut

Doctor integrated into hospital's functioning (theatre, staff, equipment) = vicarious liability.

  1. Note hospital provides theatre, staff, equipment.
  2. Doctor visits regularly (twice a week).
  3. Integration triggers vicarious liability; choose integrated option.
Control Exception Mnemonic~5smnemonic

Mnemonic: 'Hospital's Duty Stays' (HDS) – hospital liable despite independent contractor if duty non-delegable.

  1. Recall mnemonic: 'Hospital's Duty Stays' (HDS).
  2. HDS = Hospital liable despite independent contractor if duty non-delegable.
  3. Match to option B.
